In 1935 Bon Maharaja returned from his preaching efforts in Europe, and you were seated among the assembled Vaiṣṇavas to receive him. Such an auspicious gathering! The mood was one of great hope and jubilation. Following in the footsteps of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī desired to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the whole world, and with constant appeals he encouraged his followers to do it. Previously, the supreme teachings and mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu were confined within the boundaries of India, but now those boundaries had suddenly been breeched, and it seemed that a great wave of Kṛṣṇa consciousness would soon inundate the Western world.

Not long thereafter, however, the untimely disappearance of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura descended like dense fog over the Gauḍīya mission, and the devotees were plunged into sadness, dismay, and confusion. In the wake of their loss, the urgency for preaching in the West somehow faded away. The world had lost its great spiritual protector, and those inroads to Europe made by Bon Mahārāja gradually disappeared. But deep within your heart, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the desire of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī remained alive, growing stronger and stronger, and the time inevitably came when there was no containing it.

As an entry in a book describing the world history of religion might read:

1965\. His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda suddenly emerges onto the world stage, and with the instructions of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura as his life and soul, he proceeds to inundate the whole world with a great flood of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. On the order of his spiritual master and directly under the shelter of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda appears in the West and chants the holy name of the Lord: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma, Rāma, Hare Hare.

The rest, of course, is history, but what a wonderful spiritual history it is, worthy even of the pages of the *grantha-rāja, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*! Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you brought to the West that most valuable gift—*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the beautiful story of the Personality of Godhead—but you also wonderfully played an extended part in those pastimes yourself by being the living example of the topmost devotee absorbed in devotional service and love of God.

So how do we thank you? Your pastimes bestow so much transcendental happiness when we hear them! You are always there for us! In the beginning! In the middle! And in the end!! Had you not come, how could we have ever begun this transcendental journey, and if not for you, how could we ever complete it? Had you not come, how could we have found sense in the world? Indeed, how could the world find any sense in itself? And how could it even survive?

Seeing the tragic condition of Kali-yuga, Mother Earth suffers so much, but somehow or other she continues to bear the pain just to facilitate the transcendental movement of your lotus feet. After all, your lotus feet are very soothing for those hankering after Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and the Earth is no exception. As long as you continue to bless her with your presence, she will bear any pain to support your transcendental endeavor.

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how do we really thank you? Perhaps we should follow the example of Mother Earth. Perhaps we can follow in her footsteps. Perhaps we can bear all the pain, separation, trials and tribulations, as long as we surrender to the transcendental movement of your lotus feet. We will do what it takes. We will even agree among ourselves. And we will beg to remain

Your eternal servants, the residents and friends of ISKCON New Govardhana, Australia.

(written by Veṇu-Gopāla Dāsa)

## New Govardhana (San Diego)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your Divine Grace, I serve you and the devotee community as the temple president of ISKCON of San Diego, and I am writing this offering on the behalf of our community. Since I am a person, an individual unit of consciousness, my knowledge and objectivity about the world outside of me is limited to my experience, the vision of reality as it looks from my corner. I pray and hope to objectively express myself on behalf of our community.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, your pleasure and satisfaction is the measure of our success. Before meeting Your Divine Grace, we were all souls drifting about the material creation, chronically and painfully lacking purpose and direction. Some of us met you in person, whereas most of us met you in the form of your books, or we met your representatives. The lack of purpose rendered our lives useless. Anything without a purpose is devoid of value and is discarded as garbage. This is the position of conditioned souls in the physical creation. To avoid existential crises, poor souls opt to distract themselves by engaging their knowledge-acquiring senses with sensual objects, their only aim being distraction. Such a life is a sad one. For such people, no matter how jolly they may look externally, deep inside there is nothing but sadness and suffering.

As a perfect conductor of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you liberate the conditioned souls from this kind of imprisonment. Though seemingly there are numerous problems in the material world, truly there is only one: the lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. To give one Kṛṣṇa is the only genuine act of compassion. Otherwise one’s compassion is like “blowing on a boil.” Being extremely merciful and tolerant, you always want to help the suffering souls. This is the heart of your movement. You helped us, and you want us to help others. Perfect arrangement! You have inherited the compassion from your Guru Mahārāja and handed it to us, and now it is our duty to hand it down to the next generation. And we must make sure they clearly understand the purpose of your movement; otherwise the chain of compassion will break.

Here at New Govardhana we are trying hard to carry on your compassionate mission and hand the mood down to the new generation. It is not easy. Many of us are not pure devotees, and our hearts are not as tender as they should be. Compassion many times comes to us as a matter of duty rather then a natural inclination. We pray to you and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa to make our hearts soft and devoid of lethargy when it comes to matters of compassion.

Conditioned souls don’t want to see the light of reality. They prefer to be blind and distracted. The few of them that do come to Kṛṣṇa consciousness may get absorbed in hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa. But those that sacrifice their comfort for the sake of spreading your compassionate mission are rare. To such glorious followers of yours we offer deepest respect.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am sure you would be very happy to see all the new *bhaktas* and *bhaktins,* the flowers of America, who have joined your movement over the last year. They have come to truly worship you for the gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you have given them. To all of us you are the light that guides us through this otherwise extremely bewildering existence. We repeatedly bow at your lotus feet, and we pledge to exert even more energy in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness this year. Please pray for us and give us your mercy so that we can act as conductors of your compassion. We hope you are happy with us.

Your servants at New Govardhana, ISKCON San Diego, California, USA.  
(written by Mahat-tattva Dāsa)

## New Navadvīpa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You have taught us that the best course, according to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, is to let people in general remain in whatever condition they are in but to engage them in hearing the instructions and activities of Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* This is true because most people are very fallen and there is no time to elevate them by a gradual process. The transcendental messages of the scriptures should be received from the lips of realized souls, and in this way persons may continue to live in whatever condition they find themselves and still make progress in spiritual life. Thus one can surely advance and fully realize the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

This is confirmed in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.14.3, wherein it is stated:

*jñāne prayāsam udapāsya namanta eva*  
*jīvanti san-mukharitāṁ bhavadīya-vārtām*  
*sthāne sthitāḥśruti-gatāṁ tanu-vāṅ-manobhir*  
*ye prāyaśo ’jita jito ’py asi tais tri-lokyām*

**“Those who, even while remaining situated in their established social positions, throw away the process of speculative knowledge and with their body, words and mind offer all respects to descriptions of Your personality and activities, dedicating their lives to these narrations, which are vibrated by You personally and by Your pure devotees, certainly conquer Your Lordship, although You are otherwise unconquerable by anyone within the three worlds.”**

In the purport, written on the basis of the *ācāryas’* commentaries, we find this statement:

The word *jīvanti* indicates that a devotee who always hears about Lord Kṛṣṇa will go back home, back to Godhead, even if he can do nothing except maintain his existence and hear topics concerning the Lord.

What a powerful statement this is! And what is even more wonderful is that we see living proof of the veracity of this statement not only in the lives of thousands of persons that have been exposed to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but in our own lives as well. So powerful are topics about Kṛṣṇa heard from a pure devotee that it is as if they forcibly drag one to ultimate success, even if one does nothing other than continue hearing.

Therefore every day we sing, *guru-mukha-padma-vākya cittete koriyā-aikya ār nā koriho mane āśā.* “My only desire is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from your lotuslike mouth.”

Many years ago you wrote a wonderful letter to all your “Sons and Daughters” in the Los Angeles temple:

If you feel at all indebted to me then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me. Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such as attitude by the disciple. In the Bhagavad-gita it is said: *vyavasayatmika buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana*, “Those who are on this path are resolute in purpose, and their aim is one.” Our only business is to be fixed up in devotional service by pleasing the spiritual master. Those who are not fixed up they have various lines of action (eka means “one’’ and bahu means “many’’).

The real ocean of mercy is Krsna, and it is the duty of the spiritual master to tell his disciple to come to the ocean and be happy. The spiritual master’s duty is to lead the disciple to this ocean. I am trying my best, and if you try to follow surely you will benefit.

Bhaktivinode Thakura has sung, “*Krsna sei tomara, Krsna dite para, tomara sakati ache. Ami ta’kangala, krsna krsna boli, dhai tava pache pache.*” “Kṛṣṇa is yours and you have the power to give Him to anyone you wish. I am poor and wretched and running behind you shouting ‘Krsna, Krsna!’”

Kṛṣṇa is unlimited, no one can catch Him, but if someone follows the parampara, He agrees to be captured.

We, your servants at New Navadvīpa Dhāma, are certainly running behind you, falling at your feet and begging for your mercy. Please release us from all material contamination and help us serve your instructions to the best of our ability. We have full faith because of our direct experience that the process of constantly hearing the words from your lotus mouth and following your instructions without deviation is the surest way to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this day we beg for your mercy. Let us rededicate ourselves to the execution of your order in cooperation with all your disciples and granddisciples in this great mission.

Your humble servants at New Navadvīpa Dhāma, Honolulu, Hawaii, USA.

## New Nilācala

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have introduced us to such a variety of festivals in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but this special day of your appearance is the most significant of them all. We want to thank you for choosing to come to us and for changing our lives completely around—from limited, complex, and mundane ones to ones that are unbounded, simple, and yet adventurous!

You have so expertly given us the complete and pure formula for progressing toward the ultimate perfection of life: rendering pure devotional service to the servants of the servants of the servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. In the wonderful assembly of devotees you have created in different centers around the world, you give each one of us the rare opportunity to practice such pure service.

In Philadelphia, the “City of Brotherly Love,” we often gather in the temple of Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā or in the temple of Śrī Pañca-tattva (downtown) and see the magic of your pure desire to please your spiritual master shine through. Amazing devotees from various backgrounds, each with their unique, fascinating talents and with a sincere desire to serve Śrī Kṛṣṇa, work together to create such an enlivening and uplifting atmosphere—true brotherly love! Of course, there are trying moments at times, but the purification and transcendental nourishment we experience in our hearts, especially during these times, make it all worthwhile.

The result of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is that one becomes increasingly enlightened, and he enjoys life with a thrill, not only for some time, but at every moment. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 18.76, purport\]

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bringing us together and for leading us forward toward such a realm of a thrill at every moment! Thank you for pushing on strongly the mission of your dear spiritual master, regardless of all the severe austerities and pains you personally had to go through. If not for your strong determination and one-pointed, intense desire to give us Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we would just be scattered in different parts of the world today, simply absorbed in dead, dull matter. Thank you for the gift of your beloved disciples who follow closely in your footsteps—their very presence is such an encouragement, and their incessant mood of selfless service to you helps us stretch ourselves out of our “comfort zones” and aspire to be like them.

We offer you our grateful hearts for giving us one another to serve and with whom to cultivate transparent Vaiṣṇava relationships. We pray to Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śaradvihārī, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva to help us be pure in thought, word, and action so we may assist you untiringly in your compassionate efforts to bring more and more struggling souls of this world to Their lotus feet, where they can experience relief and bliss. We pray to Them that we may serve together in a way that will bring a beautiful broad smile to your face! Again, thank you so very much.

Your servants at New Nīlācala Dhāma, ISKCON of Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, USA.

## New Pānihāṭi

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.1.22)

Because of your compassion for the suffering souls on this planet and your dedication to the order of your spiritual master, you have established the means by which all human beings can awaken their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore, the residents of this world are the most fortunate beings in this universe.

Your life is an example of determination to execute the order of the guru. You were a householder entangled in family maintenance, and your wife and children were not inclined to assist you in your service to Śrī Guru. You were not trained in the *āśrama* of your Guru Mahārāja. The instruction given to you to go abroad and preach was not embraced by your godbrothers. Everyone thought the order to spread the chanting of the holy name to every town and village in the world was a noble idea, but no one thought it would actually happen. In your *sannyāsa* years, you could see that time was running out. Your body was old and your bank account empty, and your attempts to create an international preaching society in India had failed. To others, it appeared that it was best for you to publish as many books in English as possible and wait for you life to end while residing in the holy *dhāma* of Vṛndāvana.

But you are one of the greatest generals in Śrī Caitanya’s army. In the midst of so many good reasons to give up on the order to preach in the West, you found a way. You had to convince the Bank of India, the freight ship owner, and the immigration office that dying in America or on the trip to America was not such a bad thing. You are a Vaiṣṇava, and your happiness lies in using every breath for the purpose of carrying out your guru’s orders. To die in Vṛndāvana while there was still the slightest chance of delivering the holy names to the West was more objectionable than dying from a heart attack on the *Jaladuta*. That is a *saṅkīrtana* warrior’s death—glorious. A true warrior lives to die on the battlefield.

After arriving in the USA, you did not sit comfortably lecturing to intellectuals while waiting for your visa to end. You went to the most degraded neighborhood in New York City, the Bowery. Why? Because you saw the slightest chance to establish this mission to deliver the holy names from there.

The people in the Western civilization do not realize their great fortune. Such a merciful and dedicated servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has saved us all. Your ISKCON mission is surviving the test of time. It is also getting the opportunity to push on through seemingly impossible struggles. This is a sign that we must be on the correct course. Our struggles are more internal. But, as you have stated, this Society can only be destroyed from the inside. Therefore Kali is attacking us at this point. But even in the midst of Kali’s attacks, ISKCON is holding together and continuing to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Here in your New Pāṇihāṭi *dhāma* we are feeling the effects of the current economic crisis. Many of your followers are in financial difficulty. But we are maintaining the activities of *prasādam* distribution (Govinda’s Dinner Club), book distribution (Weekend Warriors), the Sunday Feast, the monthly Śrīla Prabhupāda Super Saturday, and the wonderful three-day Pāṇihāṭi festival, which includes Ratha-yātrā.

We pray that you give us the strength to be strong and fearless warriors in your *saṅkīrtana* army. Lord Kṛṣṇa is giving us an opportunity to taste the nectar of serving without consideration of material comforts. In our present state, we have to look for those small opportunities that blossom into grand displays of success in spreading your mission. This is the example you set. We pray that we have the strength to succeed where it appears that we must fail. We are meditating on your example of determination to serve the order of Śrī Guru and ŚrīGaurāṅga under any condition.

Begging to remain your *saṅkīrtana* warriors,  
Your servants at New Pānihāṭi dhāma, ISKCON Atlanta, Georgia, USA.  
(written by Śikhi Māhiti Dāsa)

## New Ramaṇ Reti

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Each year this day is given to us especially to meditate on Your Divine Grace and your transcendental vision. This begins with the fact that you are, to use your own terminology, “cent per cent” Kṛṣṇa conscious. It remains hard for many of us to fathom just how committed you are to the message and the mission of Lord Caitanya. Your books, classes, and conversations reveal a certitude that is grounded in a thorough understanding of the philosophy, knowledge of the *śāstras*, practice of the process, and profound realization.

What little we know of your life before coming to the West, from writings and your own reminiscences, point to the same conclusion. Once you met your spiritual master, you were convinced about the mission of Lord Caitanya, and that conviction led to lifelong study and practice. Early on you became active in the mission, helping your godbrothers preach in Allahabad and Bombay. Although you lived outside the *maṭha,* your 1936 Vyāsa-pūjā offering to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, delivered before a gathering of disciples in Bombay, is filled with personal realization and depth of learning. After the departure of your spiritual master, you continued to assist the mission with donations and by what would become your greatest contribution, the writing and publication of Kṛṣṇa conscious literature.

By the end of the 1950s it became clear to you that in order to fulfill the request of your spiritual master you would have to come to the West by your own endeavor. The disunity of the Gauḍīya Maṭha meant that they would not subsidize you as they had others during the time of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta. You would have to do it alone. In the next few years you completed the publication of your translation of the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and then you acquired a passport, sponsorship, and a free passage to New York, and from there you proceeded on to the home of your sponsor in Butler, Pennsylvania.

The following years would produce a flowering of Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world and create hundreds of temples, farms, and centers. Each of these centers has a story of endeavor and dedication, and you are the guiding light and inspiration for all the devotees. Every devotee in ISKCON is pledged to your mission, and you are pledged to deliver them.

In a recent lecture here, one devotee questioned the speaker whether devotees who were your *dīkṣā* disciples, or who had your personal association, were special and had some advantage over devotees who had not had such opportunities. In answer to this, the speaker, your disciple Hṛdayānanda Dāsa Goswami, made the following points:

1\. On one occasion when he visited you in your quarters, you remarked that personal association with him was not very important, that more important was service to the mission. At which point Mahārāja paid his obeisances and left in order to do some service. You always emphasized that service to the *vāṇī* of the spiritual master was more important than service to the *vapu*.

2\. Service to the mission of ISKCON was the qualification for receiving your mercy. Whether one is a disciple or granddisciple, a congregational member or of another status, no matter the time or place, anyone serving the mission would be recognized by you personally and would receive your shelter.

These points are substantiated in the following letters to your disciples:

There are two—vani or vapu. So vapu is physical presence and vani is presence by the vibration, but they are all the same. So Krsna when He was physically present before Arjuna is the same when He is present before us by His vani of Bhagavad-gita. So far I am concerned, I do not factually feel any separation from my Spiritual Master because I am trying to serve Him according to His desire. That should be the motto. If you kindly try to fulfill my mission for which you have been sent there, that will be our constant association. \[Letter to Haṁsadūta Dāsa, 22 June 1970\]

You have rightly said that the best way to associate with the spiritual master is to follow his instructions. There are two ways of associating, by vani and by vapu. Vani means words and vapu means physical presence. Physical presence is sometimes appreciable and sometimes not. Therefore we should take advantage of the vani, not the physical presence, because the vani continues to exist eternally. Bhagavad-gita for example is the vani of Lord Krishna. Although Krishna was personally present 5,000 years ago and is no longer present physically from the materialistic viewpoint, still Bhagavad-gita continues. So you have correctly concluded. \[Letter to Śuci Dāsī, 4 November 1975\]

No one should feel bereft of your association. It only requires the endeavor to fulfill your mission.

When you were presented formally to the public, you insisted that we present you as the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. This was the title that you wanted emphasized, rather than that of guru or spiritual master. At the time it might have seemed that this was a rather unimportant distinction. But you personally insisted that in any public presentation—your authorship of your books, for example, or the establishment of your *mūrti* form—that your title as founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON be prominently displayed. We can understand that there are many gurus and *ācāryas*. From *śāstra* we learn that there are many categories of guru—*śikṣā, dīkṣā, vartma-pradarśaka,* etc. And there have been many *ācāryas* throughout history. But there can only be one founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON.

There are those who have tried to minimize this founder-*ācārya* title by assertions that ISKCON is not new, that it is simply part of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava line, and that Lord Caitanya and the Six Gosvāmīs are the founders of what we call ISKCON. While this argument may seem to have merit, we beg to differ. In our philosophy there is simultaneous oneness and difference. Even Lord Caitanya’s tree of Vaiṣṇavism, as described in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, has different branches, leaves, and fruit.

Consider how unlikely it is that an elderly gentleman with practically no money or support, coming close to death on an ocean voyage and arriving in that “center of the universe” Butler, Pennsylvania, would start a worldwide Vaiṣṇava movement. When we consider the story of your sojourn in New York in 1965–66 and your subsequent endeavors around the world, we have no doubt, whatever others may say, that ISKCON was founded by you and that you will forever be its preeminent *ācārya*. This we affirm emphatically. Please give us your mercy so we may continue the task of perpetuating your movement for the re-spiritualization of this world.

Your servants at New Ramaṇa-reti Dhāma, Alachua, Florida, USA.

## New Tālavan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**There’s a Place**

There’s a place where a black ribbon of a road winds its way through grassy slopes and fields of hay.  
Like the Kālindī-Yamunā, bending and turning to caress the bank of a village so far away.  
From that place he came one day; he strode about, had much to say, and finally went on his way.  
There were a few fortunate witnesses, reminders of another time, who still recall his form divine.  
Just one visit’s all it took, to keep this holy *dhāma* alive, where all God’s creatures can survive.  
Now freed from hunger, stress, and strife, he wraps his arms around us tight,  
And all the plants and creepers here are dew-kissed with compassion’s tears  
He sheds for all humanity, untainted by mere vanity—causeless, perfect sanity.  
In a world not suited for ladies and gentlemen, he said, “I will come back for you, again and again.”  
We offer all our milk and honey, ghee and grains, and all our money at your feet that softly crossed our path,  
Where the black road winds and hugs the hearts of all your family members dear.  
Thanking you is not enough. We gladly give our lives to you, who came along one day from a village far away.

\* \* \*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

It was predicted that a great personality would be born to spread the holy name all over the world. Who could have ever imagined all the nooks and crannies on this planet that you would bless with your recipe of concentrated mercy and compassion?

We at New Tālavana Dhāma are stationed in your rural outpost, struggling to fulfill your sacred mission. It is a labor of our love for you that we remain, and we pray to become decorated with the qualities that you possess so naturally—so that we might affect the whole world the way that you have affected us. We were all lost and wandering souls, once upon a time, but you picked us up and gave us *tīrthas* in which to live, magical *prasādam* to nourish us, and most of all divine, unconditional fatherly love. We pray to always nurture the loving atmosphere you have so magnanimously provided us.

In that special year of 1975 you personally came here and gave us your vision. Our spirits soared when you arrived . . . our woodlands wept when you departed. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are still here. Some devotees milk the cows, some plow the fields, some bale the hay. Your grandchildren lead the way. Please take note of your young bhajaneers, musicians, dancers, and talented actors. Many *pūjārīs* bustle around Their Lordships, Śrī Śrī RādhāRādhā-kānta and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, adorning them with flowers from our fields. The cooks are busy night and day, and many guests have found their way to this outpost claimed by you, savior of the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we cling to you, like the Mississippi wisteria vine that wraps around the pine. There is nothing better than serving you here at New Tālavana Dhāma. Storms have battered our walls but not our hearts, for they belong to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg your blessings to continue our many projects here. A guesthouse and Sunday school grow side by side, and a new community center gives life to our congregation. As we look around, many of our pioneers here are growing old. Bless us so we may do something wonderful for you in this lifetime. You live on with us here forever! You are the soft footsteps in the winter rye. You are the honey bee that magically gives the sweet nectar that enchants the mind of Cupid. You are the field of hay where all the calves are born, whose mothers’ udders drench the land. We keep the spoon you held when you were here, and all of your cooking utensils. They sit proudly by your *mūrti;* we cherish them and we worship you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Thank you again and again for this place called home! We are your children and can never let go of your hand.

Forever in your debt,  
Your aspiring servants at New Tālavana, Mississippi, USA.

## New Varṣāṇā

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It was 1975, Melbourne. We had come over from New Zealand to help with the opening of the new temple and had an opportunity to go on a morning walk with Śrīla Prabhupāda. Everyone was wrapped up, trying to keep warm in the cold Melbourne morning. There were about twenty of us rallying around Śrīla Prabhupāda, hands in bead bags, everybody chanting, one ear on the chanting and the other waiting to hear anything Prabhupāda had to say.

I stayed at the back, thinking it was a safe place, not too close, not too far—just the right distance to keep out of trouble. I was relishing the association. There we were, walking with Śrīla Prabhupāda, the one we had dedicated our lives to. The one teaching us about the soul and our relationship with the Supreme. Our minds and bodies felt as light as clouds, not a care, just relishing the moment.

Then I found myself directly behind Prabhupāda. This was a little more of an intense situation because Śrīla Prabhupāda would stop all of a sudden, and I had seen other devotees before me step on the heels of his shoes, not once but a few times. And Śrīla Prabhupāda would turn and give one of those Prabhupāda looks.

So now my thinking pattern had changed. Hand in bead bag, chanting, I should be listening to my chanting, but no, I was thinking that at any time Śrīla Prabhupāda could stop. And would I be ready to stop also, or was I to walk right onto the back of my spiritual master? In a sense I was becoming one with Śrīla Prabhupāda’s walking.

And then Prabhupāda stopped. But I stopped also, relieved that what I feared the most had not happened. Relief. Then I started to think I was the right person to be walking behind Prabhupāda. Such a small thing, but it made me feel so important. This was my service for the moment, to walk behind Prabhupāda and make sure I didn’t bump into him or step on the heels of his shoes. The last thing I wanted was to inconvenience my spiritual master.

The next moment I was standing in front of Śrīla Prabhupāda, asking him a question. I could feel my heart pounding in my chest and was becoming a nervous wreck. Then I asked the question. Everyone stopped. What had I done? This had better be a good question. All eyes and ears were on Śrīla Prabhupāda and me. “Śrīla Prabhupāda, has the material world always existed?”

“Yes,” he replied, “just like hunger, sometimes manifested, sometimes not.” He smiled.

My heart stopped pounding and my nervousness subsided. I felt the connection had been made. Personal contact, the wait of a million births ended. A moment in time, a moment to relish, a moment to always remember. A gift from Kṛṣṇa. Thank you.

Your servants at the New Varṣāṇā farm community, Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Kalasaṁvara Dāsa)

## New Vraja-dhāma (Hungary)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of SarasvatīGosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

In the name of all the devotees at New Vraja Dhāma, a model Kṛṣṇa conscious village and farm community, please let me greet you on this wonderful occasion of your appearance day.

It is 2009. The world is in the middle of the worst economic crisis since the Great Depression. Environmentalists, scholars, and other professionals are saying that we are not just facing but are already experiencing climate change. The world is facing severe draught, shortages of water for agriculture and drinking, food crises, and so on. Many people do not see or do not want to face these crises, but there is no question that we are suffering from them now. It is not that they will happen only to our children or grandchildren; they are happening to us. You spoke about these crises thirty-five years ago:

There will be no rainfall, and there, food scarcity and taxation by government, income tax. Income tax is to plunder. . . . This is Kali-yuga. So in this position of harassment, how the peaceful *varṇāśrama* can be revived? It is very difficult. It is almost impossible. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.25.14, 14 November 1974, Bombay\]

You were very much interested in changing the direction of the world’s industrial economy. You wanted to see a move toward a more simple and natural way of life. You wrote extensively about the shortcomings and problems associated with industrial economies, and you expressed a desire that ISKCON develop an alternative economic system based on simple living and high thinking. You wanted members of ISKCON to establish rural communities based on Vedic spiritual principles. You said many times that you wanted the message of simple living and high thinking to be taken seriously by educators, government officials, and other leaders of human society.

Following your desire, the devotees in New Vraja Dhama are making a humble attempt to establish and develop a sustainable model of Vedic village life for the broad human society, all based on the text of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and your purports. By scientifically developing a model of environmental, economic, and social sustainability, we have found that more and more educators, professors, scholars, politicians, and other leaders of society are accepting that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, what you have founded, is lighting the way out of Kali-yuga’s darkness.

His Grace DrutakarmāPrabhu, your foremost preacher in scientific circles, recently visited New Vraja Dhāma and remarked:

This type of presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness has brought ISKCON into the highest educational and government circles concerned with ecology and sustainable lifestyles in Hungary, where we are offered the chance to bring their attention to our unique Vedic perspective on these matters. . . . ISKCON is now seen by many influential persons as making a significant contribution to the solution of one of the world’s major problems. This is exactly the kind of reputation that ISKCON should have.

Our endeavors can succeed only by your causeless mercy, by your teachings, by your books, and by the efforts of your dedicated followers. Our desire is to please you by serving and fulfilling the desires of your beloved disciple His Holiness Śivarāma Mahārāja. Thus we would like to render our service and our life to your mission and to help others change their lives and become eco-conscious, God conscious, and ultimately Kṛṣṇa conscious. We pray to you that we can serve you by making this effort a success by following in the footsteps of your elevated disciples.

The devotees at New Vraja Dhāma, Hungary.  
(written by Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Dāsa)

## New Vrindaban

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In this world devotion to the Supreme Lord is very rare.  
To receive the fellowship of Vaiṣṇavas  
Cannot be imagined even in dreams.  
To honor *prasādam*, which makes one mad after God,  
Is simply unfathomable for most.  
The sublime pleasure of living and serving in the holy *dhāma*  
Is always beyond anyone’s faintest hope.  
The nectar and intoxication of the holy names  
Is something that has only been attainable by a select few.  
And above all, to receive the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa  
From the lips of a pure devotee  
Is certainly the rarest and most glorious experience in this world.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your causeless compassion cannot be properly understood. How is it that in your old age you left your precious Vṛndāvana and single-handedly flooded the world with these rare gems, making them accessible to all? Is it because your mercy and kindness are immeasurable?

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,

By your desire New Vrindaban has entered its fifth decade. Most of the thousands of alternative communities which began in the 60’s and 70’s have fallen by the wayside.

But your devotees, who have endured so much, continue to remain steady and move forward. Now, in this time of economic insecurity (as if anything in this whole material world is ever secure) your instructions for New Vrindaban are more important than ever.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are the eternal guide and well-wisher of your devotees. Our only hope in this world is to mold our lives so that we may somehow please you. We beg that we may continue to serve you by serving the pilgrims who visit New Vrindaban, by honoring the Vraja-vāsīs properly, by chanting the holy names with great attention, and by always remaining at the lotus feet of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vṛndāvanacandra.

*Kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyathā.*

The inmates of New Vrindaban Community, West Virginia, USA.  
(written by Saṅkīrtana Dāsa)

## New York (Brooklyn)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

*śrī guru caraṇ kamal bhaja mana,*  
*guru kṛpā binā nahiṁ koi sādhana-bala,*  
*bhaja mana bhaja anukṣaṇa*

**“O mind, just worship the lotus feet of the bona fide spiritual master. Without receiving the mercy of the pure devotee, one will not have the potency to execute regulative spiritual practices. Therefore, O my dear mind, I urge you to worship him. Please worship him at every moment.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, one meaning of the great honorific title *Prabhupāda* is “the master at whose feet all other masters sit.” There are significant and great personalities of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava lineage who are such masters at whose feet all other masters sit. The great preceptor Lord ŚrīBrahmā is such a great Vaiṣṇava *ācārya,* a self-realized *prema-bhakti* empowered master, as is his divine son, the great spiritual master of *everyone,* Śrī Nārada Muni. And Śrī Nārada’s very exalted, empowered disciple Śrīla Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Veda-vyāsa is one as well, as is the great *ācārya* and master Śrī Madhvācārya, after whom our *sampradāya* is, in part, named.

In the *nitya-līlā* of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord ŚrīNityānanda Rāma, we find many great Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* spiritual masters, such as Śrīla Mādhavendra Purī, Śrīla Īśvara Purī, and Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda. We also hear of the wonderful spiritual attributes of great *nitya-siddha* Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* masters such as Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī, Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī, Śrīla Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmī, and Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī. In addition, we hear from and become directed and elevated in our Kṛṣṇa consciousness by great *prema-bhakti* Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* masters such as Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī and Śrīla Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura. His Divine Grace Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura continues a super-potent line of great Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* masters, who each possess awesome spiritual power and at whose feet all other masters sit.

By your mercy upon us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we regularly relish nectar from the great *nitya-siddha* Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* master of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s *nitya-līlā* His Divine Grace Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī. Among those in the recent era, we hear of the glorious devotional qualities and the wonderful prayers and teachings of such great Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* masters as Śrīla Jagannātha Dāsa Bābājī, Śrīla Sac-cid-ānanda Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Śrīla Gaurakiśora Dāsa Bābājī, and the great ray of Viṣṇu His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda 108 Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta SarasvatīGosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda, all great self-realized *nitya-siddha prema-bhakti* Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* masters. All of these great souls are extraordinary spiritually perfected devotees and great masters at whose feet all other masters sit.

Many years ago, by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, we received the priceless good fortune of attending Ratha-yātrās that you blessed with your transcendental presence and at which you spoke about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we heard you present Kṛṣṇa consciousness at *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lectures, and we saw your ecstatic loving mood at Deity installations. On morning walks with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we had a confidential close-up of your powerful devotional qualities. Deity greetings and *guru-pūjās* with wonderful ecstatic *kīrtanas* were a perfect place to see your elevated level of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to feel the amazing ecstatic effects your potent spiritual vibration had on our hearts. Although many of us spent only a relatively short time in your physical presence, the powerful spiritual impression this nonetheless made on our consciousness, as well as the powerful spiritual effects on our body, left no doubts in many disciples’ minds regarding the elevated position of Your Divine Grace as the self-effulgent ISKCON founder-*ācārya,* the great *nitya-siddha prema-bhakti* Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* master, like so many wonderful great Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* masters who appeared before Your Divine Grace and who are all masters at whose feet all other masters sit.

We knew since our earliest days as your insignificant menial servants that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are in that same league of elevated *nitya-siddha* great Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* spiritual masters as those esteemed members of our Brahma-Mādhva-Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya*. No person within this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement or outside of ISKCON need ever doubt your elevated position in Kṛṣṇa consciousness as a confidential eternal associate of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

One thing I am puzzled about, though, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is this: If the meaning of *Prabhupāda* is “the great master at whose feet all others masters sit,” then how is it that an ignorant, insignificant fool such as me ever got to sit at your lotus feet even for a moment? Your Divine Grace must certainly be the pure devotee representative of the all-merciful Lord ŚrīNityānanda Rāma or the all-merciful Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing such an unqualified fool as me to get close to you and thus making my life very special and my commitment to Your Divine Grace eternal, as I beg to remain your menial servant forever.

On this most blessed auspicious occasion of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, I know you must be feeling wonderful bliss and love in your elevated state of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please hear and tolerate my humble request: If you so desire, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless all the devotees at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandira with your mercy and love, and after blessing everyone, if any mercy remains, please consider blessing your foolish insignificant servant with a small drop of your special mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Thank you very much for delivering us to the platform of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the great Vaiṣṇava founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. You are indeed Prabhupāda, the great master at whose feet all other masters sit. All glories to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindadeva! All glories to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi! And all glories to all the menial servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandira, Brooklyn, New York, New York, USA.  
(written by Rāmabhadra Dāsa)

## New York (Manhattan)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and ŚrīGaurāṅga Mahāprabhu!

On this most auspicious and wonderful day of your appearance, on behalf of the Manhattan community I would like to express my deepest gratitude to you for bestowing upon us the inconceivable and unimaginable gift of pure devotional service to the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Muralīdhara and Their dedicated servants.

We just experienced an amazing festival in which the *choṭa* Rādhā-Muralīdhara Deities from Cleveland were reunited with our Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Muralīdhara Deities, whom we worship here in the East Village of Manhattan, just a block from your first temple at 26 Second Avenue. These Deities were installed under your care. The festival was our biggest and best ever. There was no dry eye in the audience. Hour after hour we sat in rapt attention, hearing about the exalted love and dedication offered to Their Lordships by Their surrendered servants throughout the decades.

The star of and inspiration behind this festival was your very dear disciple Mother Kāmagiri, who traveled with her children and transcendental associates from Cleveland.

The *brahmacārīs* at the Bhaktivedanta Ashram here in New York have been trying to serve Their Lordships for the last two years to the best of their ability. But hearing and seeing the example of Mother Kāmagiri and her associates has left us speechless and has permanently altered our relationship with Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Muralīdhara. Seeing the devotion of these exalted souls thoroughly humbled us and gave us a glimpse of what true selfless service is. Witnessing the selfless devotion of these ladies and the transformative power they carry, we realized that we are just beginners, standing at the threshold of *bhakti*. This is a wonderful feeling, because it puts us in touch with our constitutional position of being the servant of the servant of the great souls who have dedicated their lives to the pleasure of Guru and Gaurāṅga and who are very, very merciful. Service to their feet grants permanent freedom from material anxieties and desires.

This is such an invaluable gift, and we would like to thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bestowing it upon us. This festival was a wonderful example of how you have transformed the lives of your disciples forever. Even though we have never seen or experienced you in this physical realm, your presence was felt very strongly throughout, because you have entered the hearts of your disciples and whenever they speak they reveal you.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have inherited this impossible mission of awakening pure love of Kṛṣṇa in the hearts of whomever we meet by becoming instruments in your hands. On this auspicious day I fall at your lotus feet and beg, although completely unqualified, for a drop of your divine grace to infuse our hearts so that we may become qualified to assist you in your glorious mission.

Your aspiring servants at Bhaktivedanta Ashram, New York, New York, USA.  
(written by Yajña-puruṣa Dāsa)

## Oslo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust at your lotus feet.

We are just a few devotees trying to serve your mission up here near the Arctic Circle in Oslo, Norway. As I am writing this offering at the end of March, I can still see half a meter of snow outside the window. When somebody told you that our neighbor Sweden has the highest suicide rate in the world, you once remarked that this was not surprising for a place where one doesn’t see the sun the whole winter.

But the amazing thing is that by trying to follow your instructions we feel that we are not living in Norway but are with you, that you have empowered us to establish an “embassy of the spiritual world” in this most unlikely of places. This is the practical proof of your purity—that if we simply try to follow what you have given we have no problems and feel Kṛṣṇa’s presence in a very powerful way.

Your life itself is an inexhaustible source of inspiration. Nobody can really understand what austerities you undertook to start the ISKCON movement. You were struggling alone for many, many years, seemingly meeting defeat after defeat, and yet you never gave up. Compared to that, the difficulties we are going through seem puny. At the same time, we feel proud to be in the same “club” as you, the club of pioneer preachers who, if necessary, are willing to sacrifice even their lives for Lord Caitanya’s mission.

At the end of your last visit to New York, as you were leaving, one devotee desperately implored you to go to Gītā-nāgarī for some rest instead of embarking on another ordeal of hectic travel. He rightly perceived that you would not stay much longer in this world unless you took it easy. Your answer, as you were walking out of the elevator on the ground floor of the temple, is booming in our heads: “I want to die on the battlefield of preaching, just like Arjuna.” There can be no doubt that you could have lived much longer, had that been your concern.

You gave your life for us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. What more proof do we need of your love? The sage Dadhīci, when requested by the demigods, willingly gave them his own bones so they could use them to make a thunderbolt for killing Vṛtrāsura. In a similar way, you willingly spent your life force to make a breach in the wall of Kali’s fortress, so that all the preachers following in your footsteps could enter and engage in “undercover” infiltration.

And you assured us that by dedicating everything to the preaching mission, we ourselves would feel unbounded bliss, despite whatever difficulties might arise. At the end of your stay with us, one little pastime demonstrated this in a vivid way. At the end, being extremely weak, you had to be carried to the balcony at the back of your quarters in Vrindavana, where you spent your mornings basking in the sun. As you were being carried up the stairs one morning, you started laughing uncontrollably, your whole emaciated body shaking. After coming to the balcony, you told your amazed disciples the story of the poor man who ate only grasshoppers but rode a white horse when going to pass stool. They didn’t understand the joke at first. Where does the white horse come into the picture? Those who are familiar with Indian village life can better understand, since the toilet is in reality simply a field, sometimes at some distance from one’s house.

Anyway, the point to observe is how even though you were on the verge of leaving your body, you were in such a joyous mood. What a powerful example of someone who is fully realized, who understands fully that he is not this miserable body but an eternal, blissful servant of the most beautiful Supreme Personality of Godhead, our all-powerful, all-merciful Lord Kṛṣṇa! You gave life to the idea of transcendence, gave us faith that we can also attain it. You illuminated our dark, miserable lives and turned hopelessness into hope. You taught us that being a servant of Kṛṣṇa and his pure devotee is the most exalted position, which we should strive for by any and all means.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Your servants in Oslo, Norway.

## Panama

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

We here in Panama are a small group, but by your mercy Kṛṣṇa consciousness is going on nicely here. Although you never visited us personally, you mentioned Panama in a few of your letters, and just that you thought about us makes us feel fortunate. You are present here in the form of your books, teachings, and *mūrti,* and in fact everything that we do is to try to please you. We hope that you are pleased with our tiny efforts, and we will always continue to do more and more for your pleasure.

We are very happy to tell you that with the help of our tiny congregation we have been able to put on five Ratha-yātrās. All have been very successful, and we want to continue doing this, as we know that you have given us this mercy by introducing Ratha-yātrā to the Western countries.

We have also participated in Panama’s Annual Christmas Parade, which is attended by half a million people. Everyone got the chance to take your *darśana* and Lord Jagannātha’s.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it’s a privilege to get a chance to glorify you. There are so many things that can be said. Every year devotees write of you, and we are always very eager to read and relish your pastimes and hear of your achievements. We have seen that any devotee who has had any little pastime with you always repeats it over and over without getting tired, and we also keep on hearing and hearing it without getting tired.

You are our most beloved, although we are all useless. But having you on our front line gives us respect and unimaginable love. We hope we have your blessings for whatever preaching endeavors we undertake. All of us here at your ISKCON Panama *yātrā* fall at your feet and offer our most humble obeisances. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Panama.

## Pandharpur

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We at ISKCON Pandharpur would like to tell you about the developments here. In the past year we have really strived to improve the conditions for the devotees in the *āśrama*. By you mercy we have realized the need for boiling down the milk. We have commenced regular *bhakta* training and counseling, and the *sādhana* of all the devotees has improved a lot. Due to the increased study of your instructions, we are gaining spiritual strength to overcome more obstacles and work at a higher speed and with a broader vision.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are so thankful for all the things you have given us.

In the coming year we plan to observe many major festivals, such as Kṛṣṇa-Janmāṣṭamī and Diwali. But the most spectacular should be the celebration of the fifth anniversary of the installation of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pandharināth. The presence of the Deities has meant that so many people who are coming to take *darśana* of Śrī Viṭhṭhala also come to our ISKCON temple, get your books, and hear about chanting *japa* and other aspects of devotional service. So we are anticipating that many, many devotees from all over Maharashtra will visit Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pandharināth on their fifth anniversary.

Also, since ISKCON Pandharpur is the base for the All-India Padayātrā, we will make a great festival to celebrate the silver jubilee of the All-India Padayātrā, which His Holiness Lokanāth Swami started in 1984 on your instruction. The All-India Padayātrā team will be here, and thousands of devotees from all over the world will come to Pandharpur to take part in the celebrations.

We are planning a three-day festival with many features and programs. We will also publish a special issue of the Padayātrā Newsletter, which we will distribute to all ISKCON centers around the world to inspire more Padayātrās worldwide.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we understand in our small way how wonderful you are and how empowered you are. Only a person who has the mercy of Kṛṣṇa can do what you did and give what you gave. Please bless us with eternal devotional service unto Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pāndharināth and keep us always at your lotus feet.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants at ISKCON Pandharpur, India.

## Paris

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

With all veneration and admiration, we bow down at your lotus feet on this very auspicious anniversary of your appearance in this world. It is a day where we can strongly and wholeheartedly increase our remembrance of your divine activities.

This remembrance is so important, for it brings us closer and closer, deeper and deeper to your instructions and to your mission, which you executed by following the orders of your divine guru and Lord Caitanya’s will. This preaching mission, of which you once said, “It all started when I was living in the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndāvana,” carried you all the way to the USA in the mid 60’s and to France at the beginning of the 70’s. How fortunate this land has now become, having received your pure instructions and teachings!

May we and the whole world always remember you.

Your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, was known as the *siṁha-guru* because of his bold and fearless preaching of the glories of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, this title also applies to you. We remember your bold preaching when you said in 1973, “So Paris is a most important city of the world. Work with great enthusiasm and make Kṛṣṇa more prominent than Napoleon and the Eiffel Tower.” Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you truly have the real eternal vision.

May we and the whole world always remember you.

Before you came to the Western world you were already actively dedicating all your time and energy to the preaching mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu—performing *nāma-saṅkīrtana* and fearlessly going from door to door getting people to recover or deepen their original love for Kṛṣṇa, which you so affectionately called Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Boldly going to meet so-called influential people, you advised them to stop wasting their time in illusory activities and take up the real *sanātana-dharma*.

May we and the whole world always remember you.

Then you came alone to save us, crossing the ocean at a very advanced age. Who could have done what you did? So many times you asked your godbrothers to come and help your preaching. But none came. Actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that Kṛṣṇa was always on your side and never left you. Kṛṣṇa does not give such a blessing to ordinary people but only to those pure devotees who follow the example of Prahlāda Mahārāja by remembering Kṛṣṇa in all circumstances and preaching His glories without any fear.

May we and the whole world always remember you.

We pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our divine *siṁha-guru,* that you always allow us to continue our efforts to expand your divine mission and that you remove all obstacles to our reaching the lotus feet of our Supreme Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā Paris-Īśvara.

Your grateful and aspiring servants in Paris, France.

## Pecs

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Before I write my annual offering to you, I try to meditate on you, on your qualities. The first thing that comes to my mind is your compassion for the living entities. When Śrī Caitanya was in Rāmakeli, he predicted:

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*

**“In as many towns and villages as there are on the surface of the earth, My holy name will be preached.” (*****Caitanya-Bhāgavata, Antya-khaṇḍa*** **4.126.)**

The names of Kṛṣṇa give the ultimate solution for the problems of mankind. But who was going to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s prediction? Who was going to distribute these names to the world?

In 1896 Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura planted the seed by sending copies of his booklet *Shree Chaitanya Mahaprabhu: His Life and Precepts* to McGill University, in Canada. Later his son, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, continued the mission of Lord Caitanya. He sent a few of his disciples to Western countries, but they could not do much. Then who was going to fulfill the prediction of Lord Caitanya?

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you had a godbrother named Dol Govind Shastri. He used to teach Sanskrit in the same building where you lived as a householder in Calcutta after your marriage. One day you were sitting outside the building chanting *japa,* and Dol Govind had just completed giving his class and had come out.

“Hare Kṛṣṇa, Abhay Babu!” he said, “Did you know that Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura said that one day Russians, Germans, French, etc., would chant the names of Gaurāṅga in *saṅkīrtana*?”

“Yes,” you replied.

“So how—.”

“You have doubt?”

“No, no, no. How can I doubt the Ṭhākura? But how?”

“Oh, some surrendered soul among Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatīs disciples will do it,” you said. Then as you stood up and began to walk up the steps to go inside, you turned and said, “Not you.”

“Huh?” thought Dol Govind. “What did he mean by ‘Not you’?”

You had used the word *you* in a plural form, as if to say “Not you all.” So Dol Govind Shastri was quite confused and sat down and thought, “Does that mean *he* will do it!?”

Years went by and Dol Govind lost touch with you. He moved to Cuttack. One day as he was walking he saw at a distance a huge white man sitting in in a rickshaw, wearing a *dhotī,* neck beads, and Gauḍīya *tilaka*. Dol Govind immediately ran to him, stopped the rickshaw, and began to shake him. “Who are you?” he asked. “Where are you from? What . . . ” He was so shocked that he was not even coherent. The white man said, “I am a disciple of Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.”

“Who?”

Then the devotee pulled out a BTG and showed a picture of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. “He is a disciple of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Mahārāja Prabhupāda,”

“Abhay Babu!! Oh my Lord!! You did it!!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you did it! No one else could do it! You fulfilled the prediction of the Lord and distributed Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the whole world. Now we, your followers, are trying to continue your work. Please give us the intelligence to be able to preach. From your example we understand that preaching means not only distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness but also taking care of those who accept spiritual life. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the intelligence to be able to establish *varṇāśrama-dharma* properly in the society.

Your insignificant servants at Krisnaudvar, ISKCON Pecs, Hungary.  
(written by Khoda Nitāi Dāsa)

## Perth

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

In 1944, in an article in *Back to Godhead,* you wrote:

The leaders of nations have . . . opened many factories for manufacturing weapons for the art of killing, but none has opened a factory to manufacture weapons protecting man from the cruel hands of death, although our wishful desire is always not to die.

As the materialistic nations are falsely proud of their arsenals of tanks, submarines, bombers, and nuclear weapons, which merely hasten death, so the devotees are very grateful for the weapons you have given us for stopping death, such as the holy name, temples, *prasādam,* the Vedic literatures, *japa* beads, *mṛdaṅgas,* Tulasī-devī, and *kīrtana*.

In this regard, we are pleased to report that in the last twelve months, by your mercy, we have been able to purchase an authorized temple where the residents of Perth can freely congregate to hear about the glories of Śrī Kṛṣṇa and his pure devotee.

Twenty years ago, in 1989, the Vyāsa-pūjā book listed only eight devotees in Perth, so we feel that by your potency great progress has been made. Qualitatively it is harder to measure, but on this auspicious occasion we pray that you help us make better use of the weapons you have given us for stopping death.

Your humble servants in ISKCON Perth, Australia.

## Phuket

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Here in Phuket we are very happy to have a new center of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for your divine pleasure.

Phuket is a popular tourist resort, with scenic beaches and the tropical climate of South Thailand that attract tourists from all over the world. Since the world finds its way here, why should the *saṅkīrtana* movement not be here? By your divine mercy we now have a nice place to base our preaching, and we ask Your Divine Grace to bestow your blessings on us so that we may use this center nicely and represent your ISKCON society in a manner that will bring you pleasure.

May we always remember that whatever we have is all your mercy and for your service. Never allow us to think that we are independent of Your Divine Grace, and please continue to bless us.

Your aspiring servants eternally,  
The devotees of ISKCON Phuket, Thailand.

## Prague

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Another year is behind us, and at this most auspicious time of your Vyāsa-pūjā day we have another opportunity to meditate on your mercy, which you are continuously showering upon us. On this day we can glance back at the past year and review our efforts to fulfill your desire that this Kṛṣṇa conscious movement spread all over the world.

Another year in this dejected Kali Age is behind us, and with amazement and gratefulness we can only thank you again and again that you gave us such a simple and wonderful way of life, by which we can very happily and meaningfully live our short lives in this sick world of Māyā. Māyā is always attacking us with a wide variety of weapons. Her goal is to keep the conditioned souls in the hellish life of this material world by holding them in the grip of lust, anger, and greed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us three especially powerful weapons by which we can very easily escape Māyās control: Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam,* and your books. By taking advantage of these gifts and distributing them to others, we can save both ourselves and other conditioned souls from the clutches of Māyā. You often emphasized that preaching activities that distribute these three gifts are fully sufficient, most effective, and in fact the only weapons in our war with Māyā. Here are some examples of your statements on these weapons from your letters:

If we simply stick to this programme as I have done it since the beginning, namely, kirtana, preaching, kirtana, distribution of prasadam—if you do like this only that will be sufficient. \[Letter to Sudāmā Dāsa, November 1972\]

Actually this is our real business, preaching, chanting, distributing prasadam and book distribution.” \[Letter to Sudāmā Dāsa, February 1973\]

Book and prasadam distribution, Sankirtana and Deity worship is our only business so these programs must remain energetic. \[Letter to Jagadīśa Dāsa, December 1973\]

Our business should just to be to follow the regulative schedule, deity worship, distributing prasadam, chanting, book distribution and never mind the material danger. \[Letter to Mālatī Dāsī, January 1974\]

In your Prague temple we are continuously trying to focus our attention on preaching by using these three extremely sharp weapons of book, *prasādam,* and holy name distribution. Week by week our devotees are traveling around our country distributing your books in different towns and villages. We are trying very hard to give your books to all the people we meet, whether we encounter them in their homes, the parking lots, the shopping streets, or their offices. Through various public and home preaching programs we are trying to inspire these people to read your books and to start to live according them. We are also constantly meditating on how to create more and more devotees, how to train new preachers, and how to keep our *saṅkīrtana* vans always full with *saṅkīrtana* devotees.

Twice a week in the cold months and almost every day in summer we go on a *harināma* procession in downtown Prague. These *harināmas* are extremely popular, and many, many people are attracted by the transcendental atmosphere created by the congregational chanting of the holy name. Many people spontaneously join us in the chanting and dancing without restraint.

Our Govinda’s restaurants are well known in Prague, and *prasādam* distribution has already won the hearts and stomachs of thousands of citizens, thus establishing Hare Kṛṣṇa cuisine as the main authority on vegetarian cooking.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, during this time of falsity, duplicity, and so-called open-mindedness, where there are compromises at every step, we pray to you to give us the proper intelligence by which we can discharge devotional service strictly according to your desire and expectation. Very wistfully we pray to you that we may always be able to please you by our preaching activities, and that we perform them exactly how you desire. May we always dwell in the shade of your lotus feet, and may we never blunt these sharp weapons you gave us by all-leaking liberalism.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we certainly lack many good qualities, and our endeavors are full of imperfections. We certainly commit many mistakes while we try to assist you in your mission. But please bless us that we will always keep the taste for distributing your books, that we will always keep the taste to go on *harināma* and chant Kṛṣṇa’s names loudly, and that we will flood our country with Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. May this enthusiasm and faith in your instructions be the powerful engine for our progress in spiritual life, and may we have strong conviction that simply by merging ourselves fully in the preaching service we will automatically become free of all shortcomings and naturally develop all the Vaiṣṇava qualities. May we overcome all the outer and inner obstacles by satisfying you with propagation of this *saṅkīrtana* movement.

*mantratas tantrataś chidraṁ deśa-kālārha-vastutaḥ*  
*sarvaṁ karoti niśchidram anusaṅkīrtanaṁ tava*

**“There may be discrepancies in pronouncing the mantras and observing the regulative principles, and, moreover, there may be discrepancies in regard to time, place, person and paraphernalia. But when Your Lordship's holy name is chanted, everything becomes faultless.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **8.23.16)**

Your servants at the Nitāi-Navadvīpacandra Mandira, ISKCON Prague, Czech Republic.

## Pune

Respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on the sacred occasion of your 113th Vyāsa-pūjā. All glories to your divine self!

By your tireless and fearless preaching at an advanced age, often in hostile environments, you planted the *bhakti-latā-bīja* in the hearts of millions of undeserving and even unwilling souls. The glory of selfless preachers like your divine self is repeatedly extolled in our Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava literatures. In his *Caitanya-śataka* (39), Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya writes:

*saṅkīrtanānanda-rasa svarūpāḥ*  
*prema-pradānaiḥ khalu śuddha-cittāḥ*  
*sarve mahāntaḥ kila kṛṣṇa-tulyāḥ*  
*saṁsāra lokān paritārayanti*

**“All the devotees of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya are great** ***mahātmās*****. They are the personification of the bliss of** ***saṅkīrtana,*** **and they are as good as Kṛṣṇa. They deliver the fallen souls, who are burnt by the threefold miseries, by giving them love of God.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your potency in transforming the lives and destinies of the countless souls who directly came in contact with you is astonishing and inspiring. But even more astonishing and inspiring is your potency in creating a legacy of preachers who are continuing the glorious *paramparā* you so dynamically represented and dramatically expanded. To ensure that ISKCON continues for its predicted ten thousand years the mission of compassion you inaugurated, you emphasized reaching out to and attracting the educated youth, knowing that they would be the future leaders of your movement. Your authorized biography, *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta* (Chapter 27: India Revisited: Part 1) describes one moving incident illustrating how important you considered youth outreach programs:

From Boston, Satsvarūpa wrote that he and the other devotees there were moving from an apartment to a rented storefront near Boston University. The first time Satsvarūpa entered the new storefront, he found on the floor an aerogram from Swamiji, dated October 6 from Delhi.

\[Swami had written:\] “I can understand that you have secured a very nice place in Boston and there is a very good possibility of pushing our movement amongst the student community there. Our movement is certainly very much appealing to the younger section of your country and if we are successful in the matter of attracting the student community in your country certainly this movement will scatter all over the world and fulfill the foretelling of Lord Caitanya that in every village and every town of the world the Lord will be famous for His glorious sankirtana movement. Please try for this with your heart and soul and your life will be a successful mission.”

The letter was as good as Swamiji coming personally to open the storefront and begin the preaching.

Your immense eagerness for youth preaching—as evinced by the above incident, in which you dispatched your blessings through the aerogram to the youth preaching center even before the preachers moved in—is our inspiration here at Pune.

For over a decade, we have tried to emphasize attracting youths to our movement. By the mercy that you have showered on us in reciprocation with our humble efforts, we now have 108 *brahmacārīs,* most of whom are highly educated, with degrees in engineering, medicine, or other professions. Many *gṛhastha* devotees are rendering invaluable devotional service as well, taking up preaching and managerial responsibilities, thus creating a vibrant spiritual community. This community of devotees is working unitedly to build a magnificent new temple, which we hope will serve as a base for greater outreach programs to new people and deeper training programs for devotees.

You have also blessed us with the opportunity to share with several other ISKCON centers the mercy that you have showered on us. Thus eight devotees trained in Pune have been shouldering, for several years now, significant responsibilities at your Vṛndāvana temple, which you called “the heartthrob of ISKCON.” Last year five devotees from Pune were blessed by you with leading preaching and managerial responsibilities at our center in Kolkata, which is that sacred place where you appeared and in the vicinity of which Gauḍiyā Vaiṣṇavism itself appeared. To facilitate and assist other ISKCON temples in their efforts at youth preaching, we just concluded the third Youth Preacher’s Training Course (YPTC) in two phases, with 68 youth preachers from India and abroad receiving comprehensive training in the first phase and 54 in the second.

With more and more temples earmarking dynamic preachers and significant resources for youth preaching, and more and more educated young devotees—both *brahmacārīs* and *gṛhasthas*—taking up preaching responsibilities, the future of your movement is bright.

We seek your blessings to play our insignificant roles in your ISKCON, as it grows from strength to strength and ultimately fulfills your bold prediction: “The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will go down in history as having saved mankind in its darkest hour.”

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī Mandira, ISKCON Pune, India.

## Radhadesh

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The Radhadesh-*vāsīs* beg to offer our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, which for many decades have been extending boundless mercy and shelter to your disciples and followers, as well as to thousands of visitors.

Although in your manifest pastimes you never blessed Radhadesh with the touch of your lotus feet, it is an open secret that you are present here to the extent that we endeavor cooperatively to follow your instructions and example. In fact, you have so perfectly established Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s devotional program that it is practically impossible not to feel your presence at every step.

Every morning, after undergoing the devastating effect of ignorance resulting from the necessary evil of sleep, we have the opportunity to “re-create our universe” by following the simple and sublime *tapasya* you have handed down to us, and in which you continue to lead us.
