Before *maṅgala-ārati* there is always a sign on the temple-room door that reads “Prabhupāda is being dressed.” Thus each day starts with the opportunity to be grateful for your remaining in our midst, accepting our personal service, and allowing each of us to feel our personal connection with you, despite Lord Kṛṣṇa’s having removed you from our limited vision so many years ago. As the time for *maṅgala-ārati* approaches, we repeatedly check to see if the sign has been removed, and when it is, the door is finally opened and we happily enter the temple to offer you our obeisances, touch your lotus feet, and beg for the strength to serve you as you deserve to be served. When the conchshell blows, heralding *maṅgala-ārati,* and the altar-curtains slowly open, we offer obeisances, exactly as you have taught us and as if you are personally leading us, first to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, then to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha and Their *sakhīs,* and then to Jagannātha and His transcendental brother and sister. Each item the *pūjārī* offers to Their Lordships is first offered to Your Divine Grace as *mahā-prasāda*, and then the assembled devotees are privileged to honor the *mahā-mahā-prasāda*. Without partaking in this continuous shower of mercy, the details of which we may tend to take for granted or view as routine ritual, what chance would we have to continue the practice of *sādhana-bhakti,* meant to ultimately lead us to unconditional loving service to you and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa?

Among the many instructions you gave us, your emphasising the simple practices of rising early and attending the morning and evening programs and cooperating with one another to prove our love for you, are the most essential and, seemingly, the most difficult to follow. Of course, by your mercy we are having some success, but each of us knows that if we would put aside all personal interest and independence and unreservedly dedicate ourselves to Your Divine Grace, we could achieve so much more in spreading your divine mission. On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we plead at your lotus feet that we may individually and collectively be blessed to advance in being surrendered to your instructions so that the Radhadesh community will make progress toward being the kind of offering that will satisfy you in all respects.

The servants of your servants in Radhadesh, Belgium.

## Richmond Hill/Markham

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day, your Vyāsa-pūjā,  
We humbly bow down before you in awe,  
For there is no doubt that you are the greatest *ācārya*.  
Without you our journey to Kṛṣṇa would be impossible;  
We would still be living in an illusionary bubble,  
Not knowing that the materialistic pursuit of happiness is unsustainable.  
You selflessly became our soul agent;  
Delivering us was your goal and you were confident;  
You would not allow anything to be your deterrent.  
We were blindly living in the mundane,  
But you would kindly repeat to us again and again  
That the only way out of this misery is *kṛṣṇa-prem*.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so ever-gracious;  
You generously gave us something so precious;  
For our change in path back to Kṛṣṇa, you were the impetus.  
You told us to wake up—“*Jīv jāgo*!  
Realize that your souls are sleeping from long ago!”  
This spell of *māyās* illusion we must forego.  
You said to use everything in the service of Kṛṣṇa,  
Giving examples from the *Bhāgavata Purāṇa,*  
Explaining that all can be used, from technology to a diploma.  
You instilled your instructions in your morning walks and lectures,  
In your books, magazines, and letters,  
And in your numerous questions and answers.  
You inspired through beautiful Vedic paintings,  
Hundreds of pictures, songs, and tape recordings,  
And in each and every one of your memories.  
You built so many temples, farms, and schools,  
Provided us with all the required tools,  
Built a house in which the whole world could live under Vaiṣṇava rules.  
You pulled us away from the clutches of *māyā,*  
Bringing us everything from *kīrtana* and Lord Caitanya  
To joyous celebrations such as Janmāṣṭamī and Ratha-yātrā.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are glorious!  
You were the only one to transform *karmīs* to become pious;  
In your mission you were victorious.  
You were perfect and an expert in every way and everything;  
You showed by example, from playing *mṛdaṅga* and singing  
To the proper way of worship and even cooking.  
You repeatedly said that you are our well-wisher;  
Like a father, you were our perpetual protector;  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are truly our spiritual master.  
From planting in us the *bhakti-latā* seed  
To giving us the foolproof Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra and *japa* beads,  
You gave us everything we needed to succeed.  
You ensured future success of the movement to the masses,  
Telling us to distribute books—that your books are the basis,  
Encouraging people to abandon the never-ending material races.  
By your grace, your books are available in every library,  
ISKCON is known in every country, continent, and boundary,  
And your teachings have been exposed to many a dignitary.  
Your unlimited mercy and time spent with us we cannot even measure;  
Your benevolence and blessings we will forever treasure;  
We wish to always serve for your and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, we thank you so much for giving us this opportunity;  
Thank you for showing us that we must make Kṛṣṇa our priority;  
Thank you for taking us under your shelter even though we are not worthy.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow us to remain at your lotus feet,  
Allow us to remain in the realm of ISKCON’s *mṛdaṅga* beat,  
For without you and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, our lives would be incomplete.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Gurudeva!

Your humble servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara temple in Richmond Hill/Markham, Canada.  
(written by Minakshi Devī Dāsī)

## Rome

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

We all know how dear this city of Rome is to you. When your wonderful disciple Dhanañjaya Prabhu wrote you that he and his wife had opened a new temple here, you showed great appreciation and gave them your blessings. Today, more then thirty years have passed and we are still seeking those blessings to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in an oceanic way in this famous city. Actually, here there is only a little preaching center hidden in a small street behind a famous square in the historic city center, but we know that Rome needs a big, amazing, beautiful temple able to welcome and attract the minds of the conditioned souls. We recognize that at the moment we cannot offer thousands of new devotees at your lotus feet, but we want to be able to do that in the near future. Meanwhile, please accept our little sprout of devotional service so we can continue to go on despite the big and little difficulties this project presents.

We devotees of Rome have only three spiritual desires to satisfy: to increase enormously the distribution of your books, to distribute Śrī Kṛṣṇa *prasādam* to all those we meet, and to increase the number of *harināma* parties on the streets in the city. Please continue to protect us as you have until now. We are sure that as long as we have your books as the basis, no confusion or uncertainty will be present in our daily activities.

On this sacred day we vow loyalty to your teachings. Even if sometimes they may appear unpopular or not in line with the modern times, we will still learn them by heart, present them with faith, and do all we can to distribute them widely.

We want to serve you without interruption, without asking anything in exchange. And if you desire to expand the Rome *yātrā* and multiply our activities and services, we’ll be very happy to satisfy you in full. For your pleasure, we also promise to cooperate honestly during the execution of our devotional service, despite the many differences between devotees.

All the devotees in the Rome *yātrā* thank you with deep gratitude and affection.

Your humble servants at Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Śacīnandana temple in Rome, Italy.  
(written by Līlānanda Dāsa)

## St. Louis

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our most dear and respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

As your fumbling grandchildren, we can barely come close to a glimpse of the glories of your beloved and dedicated followers, and so we find ourselves even further disqualified to approach your inconceivable glories. Yet somehow, by your special grace, we are getting this opportunity to exercise our energies in contemplating your divine presence in this world and how it is continuing to provide such deep shelter and protection to us individually and to the worldwide *saṅga* of Vaiṣṇavas. Please accept this humble offering.

The Age of Kali is degrading at such a fast pace. We were caught up in this tidal wave of nonsensical living. By some great fortune you made the huge sacrifice of leaving your place of comfort and respect to give us this gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As we think of you, we are reminded of the sun that has come to shine upon the darkness in our lives. You are brilliant in your delivery of transcendental knowledge, loving qualities, deep *śāstric* vision, and creation of a massive spiritual movement and the means to sustain such a worldwide entity. Śrīla Sūta Gosvāmī indicates that *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is the brilliant sun coming in the wake of the departure of Lord Kṛṣṇa. When values and basic human qualities took a nose-dive in the West, you brought that sun with you and began to open our eyes to the wonderful light of a loving relationship with Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given your blood in your books. You were not worried about your own body or health. Your focus was on giving each of us the gift of the Bhaktivedanta purports. Once you were experiencing excruciating pain, shaking uncontrollably, while writing in the middle of the night. But what you were concerned about was being unable to write, not your health. Who can fathom the depth of your compassion? Your books give us great inspiration and vision for our lives. The purports are your personal, loving letters to us, your dependents, just so that we may reach Kṛṣṇa. You have left the boat by which we can cross the ocean of material existence. These books, as you have taught, are Kṛṣṇa Himself incarnated in a literary form. We get to hold Kṛṣṇa in our hands!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are seated on the royal *vyāsāsana* in our temple room, carefully observing and encouraging us as we strive to serve your mission amid the various forces of the material energy acting against us. When we introduce others to you and speak of your glories, their hearts are moved by your compassion. Your example is still making devotees today, despite your apparent absence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are happy to report to you that the mood of cooperation and loving dealings is manifesting to some degree. There are more committed individuals coming to our temple, and our Sunday Feast attendance is steadily increasing. We continue to be financially solvent, with our various *prasādam* and book distribution programs as well as fundraising programs. The annual Janmāṣṭamī and Gaura Pūrṇimā festivals continue to attract new people. This year, by your inspiration, we will be organizing our first big public Ratha-yātrā! Please extend your blessings upon this event, if you so desire.

Your desire is for the unity of ISKCON, at both a local and an international level. Our part is to work on receiving your unlimited mercy and becoming qualified to establish proper loving relationships of give-and-take and love-and-trust, as you would desire, and as we are being trained by your stalwart representatives. You have clearly instructed us in the *Nectar of Instruction* (verse 5, purport):

One should not remain a *kaniṣṭha-adhikārī,* one who is situated on the lowest platform of devotional service and is interested only in worshiping the Deity in the temple. . . . One therefore has to raise himself from the position of *kaniṣṭha-adhikārī* to the platform of *madhyama-adhikārī.*

Please empower us to accept all the amazing resources you have given us so we may fully take up the life of aspiring servants and manifest loving communities that represent the values you describe in your books and personally lived by.

Aspiring to serve Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma and Śrī Śrī Nitāi–Gaura-Naṭarāja on your behalf,  
The devotees at ISKCON St. Louis, Missouri, USA.

## Salem

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, please allow us to glorify you.

We little file-and-rank servants from an insignificant spot in a remote South Indian town would like to express our gratitude in whatever capacity we can, hardly aware of your incomparable magnanimity. We are some thirty-plus souls here from five countries, and all of us are trying to cooperatively serve one common goal—actually, to serve our mutual friend, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Thanks to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are living together under one roof just because of your desire to serve Kṛṣṇa. How is it possible that somebody whom none of us have ever met in person can influence thousands of young people just like us to leave everything and take to an austere life in an *āśrama*? The youth of today are impossible. Unlike the hippies of the 60s and early 70s, who, although crazy savages, at least had the desire to abandon mainstream conformism, modern kids are just hopeless numbers in the queue for slaughter. Therefore it is a greater miracle that even today you are able to inspire young men to stop enjoying girls, cars, drugs, demonic music, and games and pursuing college degrees and careers, etc., and take up the practice of selfless devotional service. It is all because of your strong desire to serve Kṛṣṇa!

The big question is, How can we ever repay you for such a gift? You have given us freedom—freedom from false designations and wrong ideas, freedom from repeated birth and death! Is there anyone else in our lives who benefited us so greatly? No. There is nobody like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at ISKCON Salem, India.

## Santiago de Cali

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances and respect to your divine lotus feet.

In our hard struggle for material existence, we found something in the mode of pure goodness that woke us up: your books. In them we find comfort in our times of trial; they call us to not give up the struggle for self-realization, and they fill us with true happiness, which we can’t find out there in the material world. For your books we glorify you, Śrīla prabhupāda.

Your ISKCON family is the only refuge in this bleak material world; this family comes together to worship Lord Kṛṣṇa and you, and thus our children are coming to understand the real value of the life and appreciating this refuge from material confusion. For your ISKCON family we thank you and worship you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We are like children walking along in life holding your hand, trustful of your teachings. Your only desire is to take us back to Kṛṣṇa, back home, and thus you have given us your teachings so we can see the real goals we should fight for in life. For your teachings and protection we worship you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Every day, when we receive your teachings, the real meaning of everything becomes clear and we can see more and more that you are our glorious, eternal guru, life after life. For your eternal guidance and compassion we worship you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

All glories to your divine lotus feet!

Your servants at the Santiago de Cali mandir, Colombia.

## Seattle

Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You came to this material world to fulfill the prophecy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that Kṛṣṇa consciousness would spread to every town and village in the world. One such town is Seattle, where your aspiring devotees have gathered to continue your mission under the guidance of your dear disciple His Grace Harivilāsa Dāsa. By your causeless mercy, this year we are observing the first anniversary of the establishment of our new temple, and we would like to offer all the successful projects of ISKCON Seattle at your lotus feet. Even though our congregation contains very few full-time devotees and is composed mostly of *gṛhasthas* who are more or less occupied with household and family duties, by the power of your transcendental mercy we are somehow managing several successful preaching programs.

Ever since our new temple was inaugurated in July 2008, so many wonderful devotees have taken shelter of Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Nīla-Mādhava and are experiencing unlimited bliss in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The preaching activities of the temple are ever expanding, and many sincere souls are getting attracted to your movement. Through our Vedic University, formal courses on the *Bhagavad-gītā, The Nectar of Devotion,* and *Śrī Īśopaniṣad* are being offered to many devotees. The numbers of Bhaktivrikshas have increased due to the tireless preaching efforts of your granddisciples, and more and more people are taking Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously. You often expressed your view that the educated youth are the best recipients of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This year, by your blessings and mercy, as a part of our youth preaching, devotees have started a Bhakti Yoga club at the University of Washington. Through this club they are cultivating many Western-born students by conducting vegetarian cooking classes, *Bhagavad-gītā* seminars, etc. Due to the efforts of the wonderful devotees of the congregation, the activities of our Sunday school have become not only a source of inspiration for the children but also a great success in bringing many families to our Sunday Feast programs. Children engage in various cultural activities like Kṛṣṇa conscious drama, dance, story-telling, and music, all of which help them get a solid base in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You once stated that we can conquer the West with our Vedic culture. Keeping your instructions in mind, our temple, also known as the Vedic Cultural Center, is offering Kṛṣṇa consciousness to many people of Seattle through cultural presentations. Many local newspapers and magazines report on the activities of our temple, and these accounts attract more visitors. One of the main weapons we have deployed is *prasādam* distribution, and we are very happy to distribute the Lord’s mercy all over Seattle—at colleges, festivals, regional cultural events, and restaurants, and indeed at any kind of function. Many of your wonderful disciples are regularly visiting us here and giving wonderful seminars to inspire us in our Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Despite our imperfections, our temple is becoming successful because of your deep compassion and mercy for the fallen souls. Your potency is revealed whenever new devotees take initiation, whenever the temple hall becomes packed during our Sunday Feast, whenever we go on *harināma,* and whenever we distribute books and *prasādam*. To take shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet means to take shelter of His pure devotee. By taking shelter of your lotus feet, we feel completely protected and blissful. We pray sincerely to continue being your instruments in spreading your mission of the International Society of Krishna Consciousness. Please bless these children, grandchildren, and great-grandchildren of yours, who are trying to sincerely follow your instructions to purify ourselves, purify others, serve your mission, and return to the spiritual world.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your aspiring servants at ISKCON Seattle, Washington, USA.

## Siem Reap

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your loving servants!

On this most auspicious day of your appearance in this world, we feel very grateful and eternally indebted to you. Everyone must bow their head down with great respect at the dust of your lotus feet because . . .

*You have removed the burden of sin from our heart . . .*  
*You have given the whole world a way to removed the burden of sin . . .*

You teach us the essence of all the Vedic scriptures, which is rarely attainable even by great scholars . . .

*the science of Kṛṣṇa . . .*  
*the real love of the Supreme . . .*  
*the reservoir of pleasure . . .*  
*whom everyone runs after . . .*

What great service you have done! Not an easy task, more than just talk. In your advanced age, when others were drawing a pension, you began your great mission . . .

*You underwent great pain, severe pain . . .*

But with joy and full faith you carried out the order of your spiritual master, accepting it as your life and soul.

Who can measure the power of compassion you store in your heart?

You broke into dust the pride of modern Western life and revived Westerners’ loving devotional service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

You are that very personality empowered by your spiritual master, by the *ācāryas*, by Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu, by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa to lead the way back home, back to Godhead.

You came to this mortal world to fulfill the desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu:

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

The desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu is that all over the world, in every village and every town, everyone will know of Him and His *saṅkīrtana* movement. Single-handedly you made this happen.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your beloved servant has inspired us to come to Cambodia to serve your great mission—spreading the holy name of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. We realize that this is not an easy place to preach. It is full of meat-eaters, drunkards, people breaking all the regulative principles. We are just insignificant souls scattered in the vast ocean of sin. How then will it be possible to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness here? We just want to be engaged as a small instruments in your divine hand. Please make us move as you wish.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your glories are spreading widely all over the world—even here in the Kingdom of Cambodia, where we are doing our best to distribute your books. Please bestow your divine grace upon these fallen souls. Other than Your Divine Grace, who else can be our savior?

We have strong faith that the day will come when you will make these people chant the holy name of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and dance in front of the beautiful temple of Lord Viṣṇu at Angkor Vat.

Falling at the dust of your divine lotus feet,  
Your servants in Siem Reap, the Kingdom of Cambodia.  
(written by Kiśora Kṛṣṇa Dāsa)

## Silicon Valley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Worship of the *ācārya* is supremely important. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* Kṛṣṇa advises that to advance in spiritual life one must worship the *ācārya—ācāryopāsanam*. Kṛṣṇa tells Uddhava in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that one should see the *ācārya* as Himself and never disrespect him in any way. Nor, He says, should one envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.

Today, therefore, on the occasion of your divine appearance, we seek to worship you. However, we may do so only according to the extent and quality of our faith. After all, what is it that compels us to sincerely worship you or to follow your instructions? It is our *śraddhā,* or faith. The word *śraddhā* derives from the active verb *dhā,* “to place,” and *śrad,* meaning, “heart.” Its meaning therefore is “where we place our hearts.”

When asked by Lord Caitanya why he had come to see Him before going to see Lord Jagannātha, Rāmānanda Rāya replied:

The legs are like the chariot, and the heart is like the charioteer. Wherever the heart takes the living entity, the living entity is obliged to go. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 11.37\]

This is to say that our faith in you manifests itself outwardly. The state of our heart moves our legs, hands, and tongues. When our faith is pure, our service to you will be unobstructed. If we have doubts, we will hesitate and our service and attention will be diverted. A sincere disciple, therefore, works actively in devotional service while praying that the Lord remove his doubts.

Prahlāda Mahārāja prays, “O my Lord, best of the givers of benediction, if You at all want to bestow a desirable benediction upon me, then I pray from Your Lordship that within the core of my heart there be no material desires.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.10.7) Confirming this point, you write the following in your commentary to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi-līlā* 7.91:

The spiritual master is not actually happy if the disciple brings him money, but when he sees that a disciple is following the regulative principles and advancing in spiritual life, he is very glad and feels obliged to such an advanced disciple.

To help us achieve this, you asked that we scrutinize your books and other teachings, discussing them “thoroughly, threadbare.” As Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī says:

A sincere student should not neglect the discussion of such conclusions, considering them controversial, for such discussions strengthen the mind. Thus one’s mind becomes attached to Śrī Kṛṣṇa. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 2.117\]

However, the conclusions that we reach after studying your instructions must be connected carefully to *śāstra* and the example of *sādhus*. Through a single dot drawn on a page one may draw an infinite number of lines that intersect from a variety of angles. However, adding an additional dot to the same page and drawing an intersecting line through both points renders a single straight line.

As our *ācārya* and the representative of Dvaipāyana Vyāsa, you painstakingly instilled in us the habit of lining up our conclusions with evidence from *guru, sādhu,* and *śāstra*. You said:

So you have to make your progress—*sādhu-śāstra-guru vākya*. You have to corroborate whether *guru,* what *guru* is speaking, whether it is there in the scripture; what scripture is speaking, whether that is in the character of *guru,* or in the *sādhu,* saintly persons, or spiritual master. So you have to always make comparison with three things: *sādhu, śāstra, guru*.” \[Lecture. New York, 24 November 1966\]

You erected for us the pillars of *guru, sādhu,* and *śāstra* like guardrails along a winding road, to protect us on our long journey ahead after your physical departure.

One cannot modify the instructions of the *ācārya*. Rather we must modify our consciousness to follow your instructions. Until we deliberately apply your teachings and improve our standard of devotional service, we cannot be counted as your faithful followers.

You painstakingly gave us instructions intending that we eventually rise to the topmost position of devotional service. A student who remains in a lower standard or claims that he cannot become elevated certainly gives no glory to the teacher. For it is by teacher’s merciful direction that he or she advances.

On the other hand, those who dedicate their lives to the order of the *guru* become successful by his grace. And by his grace only they make others successful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to continually raise our devotional standards at ISKCON of Silicon Valley for your pleasure. On this holy occasion, we offer you our lives in service as we endeavor to fully embrace your teachings, praying to you for the strength and intelligence to do so.

Your servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, California, USA.

## Slovenia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most prostrated obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. We at ISKCON Slovenia feel most fortunate to have found shelter at your most merciful lotus feet. Thank you for your causeless mercy.

You are the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, and nobody can take your position. No one is like you. Nobody is greater then you. You are the master at whose lotus feet all other masters sit. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu especially empowered you to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world for the first time. All others can only take your remnants. We beg for the remnants you kindly left so that we can at least successfully spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Slovenia.

We try to go out every day and distribute the holy name, *prasādam,* and your amazing books. For the last few years we have also been organizing a Padayātrā, each year in a different part of Slovenia. By your divine mercy people are very receptive. They shed tears out of happiness. They are so moved. They accept mercy in the form of the holy name, *prasādam,* and books like anything. Every year we also organize a *yātrā,* or retreat, high in the mountains to improve our *sādhana*. This year the *yātrā* will focus on *kīrtana*. We also have many other preaching programs.

We also run two nice restaurants. The smaller one is called Holyfood, and the bigger one is Govinda’s. People like to visit them very much. Every day we have a lot of visitors. Most of them don’t even know they are eating *prasādam*. But they can feel and experience that this food is something special, something they have never tasted before. They dream about it and become addicted. They make loud propaganda. Please bless us that we can maintain the high quality of *prasādam* and open many more restaurants around the country.

You built the most wonderful house where everybody can happily and peacefully live in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please bless us to take nice care of the devotees, to develop the *gṛhastha* community and farm community, so that we can be more independent from modern materialistic society. Now we have a lot of newborn Kṛṣṇa conscious children, and more of them are on their way. They need special protection. Please give us your guidance so we can protect them from *māyā*.

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wrote more than forty volumes of translations and commentaries on such classics as *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and *Bhagavad-gītā*. So far we have translated only ten books into Slovenian. We need so much more of your mercy to be able to translate all the other books as soon as possible. This year we plan to publish a few new books—*The Science of Self-Realization, The Nectar of Instruction,* and so on.

We still have forty thousand stored copies of your books from the previous century. So we need your inconceivable mercy to distribute them as soon as possible. There is a great need for new *saṅkīrtana* devotees, and only by your divine mercy can they come.

Our Deity worship also needs to be improved. Śrī Pañca-tattva have kindly appeared in our temple in Their beautiful *arcā-vigraha*. Please bless us that we can correct our neglectful attitude so that we can serve Them properly and understand that They are persons, that They are personally present.

The most difficult thing in Kali-yuga is to cooperate. Please be merciful to us and give us divine vision so that we can see the good in others and be able to cooperate better.

We have so much more to do. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our lord and master! When will we be blessed with your mercy? We are fallen and devoid of all strength and intelligence. Please make us your loving servants.

We have no knowledge of our previous lives. All that we know is that after many, many lives of suffering in ignorance we have been saved by you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

How eager are we to come closer to you? It is up to us. You are ready to accept us completely, but are we ready to completely accept you?

If we examine ourselves, we will not find anything valuable. Your mercy is all that we are made of. If you are not merciful to us, we can only weep, and we will not be able to maintain our lives.

The servants of the servants of your servants at New Yogapīṭha, ISKCON Ljubljana, Slovenia.

## Solapur

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Drear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances to your lotus feet. All glories to you!

You are the dearmost devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa and are glorified all over the world. Even Lord Caitanya spread the holy name only in India, and Lord Nityānanda opened his marketplace of the holy name only in some part of India. But you, the representative of Lord Nityānanda, opened an international marketplace of the holy name. That’s why today the whole world is easily getting love of Godhead. Thousands and thousands of devotees are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa only by your mercy. Some of your disciples who have left their bodies, such as Gaura-Govinda Swami and Śrīdhara Swami, have given wonderful examples of chanting and hearing the holy name at the end. In a lecture I once heard you say *bhajan kara sādhana kara murte jānle hoy,* which means the time of death will test what kind of *bhajana* and *sādhana* one has been doing.

What a wonderful method you have given to the world—the art of dying in such a way that one will never be born again in this miserable material world.

Recently I heard a *sādhu* say in a public lecture that out of all the Indian swamis, only Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda had changed the hearts of Western people; nobody else had done this. But why just Westerners? Recently my spiritual master said to us that we must “chant for change,” so we are also trying to chant for change in our hearts and the hearts of others.

By your mercy, after struggling many years we have secured four and a half acres of land here in Solapur. Last year we built an *āśrama* for the *sādhakas*. Now we are planning to build a temple where all the congregational devotees can gather to glorify you and the Lord. The work on the *gośālā* is finally progressing, and we hope to complete it just before your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

Our congregational devotees, our *āśrama* devotees, and I are at your lotus feet. Please bless us so that we can chant the holy name purely and serve your mission properly.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants as ISKCON Solapur, India.

## Stockholm

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

When Kṛṣṇa, at the end of His Dāmodara *līlā,* decided to liberate Nalakūvara and Maṇigrīva, who were standing as two *arjuna* trees in the courtyard of Nanda Mahārāja, He was meditating like this:

Although these two young men are the sons of the very rich Kuvera and I have nothing to do with them, Devarṣi Nārada is My very dear and affectionate devotee, and therefore because he wanted Me to come face to face with them, I must do so for their deliverance. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.10.25\]

Although Kṛṣṇa as the Supersoul perfectly takes care of all living entities who have fallen from the spiritual world, He expresses in this verse that He has no business to appear before the sons of Kuvera and give them the mercy of His personal *darśana.* However, in the case of Nalakūvara and Maṇigrīva, Nārada Muni, a pure devotee of the Lord, had met them in their fallen condition on his travels around the universe and taken pity on them out of his compassion. Thus as an expert preacher, he cursed them to become trees in the courtyard of Nanda Mahārāja to remove their pride of living like celestial playboys so that, in a humble state of mind, they could have the *darśana* of Kṛṣṇa and be grateful and fully appreciate it.

Here Kṛṣṇa expresses that He, as Bhagavān, has no business giving personal attention to the fallen souls. Kṛṣṇa also makes it clear that when His pure devotee gives his compassionate attention to the fallen souls and desires their welfare, then Kṛṣṇa Himself also becomes personally concerned and enters into the lives of those fortunate souls and liberates them from material existence.

This story of Kṛṣṇa’s deliverance of Nalakūvara and Maṇigrīva by the mercy of Nārada Muni is the story of how you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your compassion, called Kṛṣṇa to appear in our otherwise condemned Kali-yuga lives. By your call, Kṛṣṇa has appeared in so many Deity forms, so many books, and so many *harināmas* and *kīrtanas* all over the world to personally enter our unfortunate lives and liberate us from out treelike existence.

Just as Nārada Muni went to Nalakūvara and Maṇigrīva, who were both naked and drunk, you went to places like The Lower East Side in New York and Morningstar Ranch in California, places that were populated by drunks, drug addicts, and naked people. By calling Kṛṣṇa’s attention, you turned these people into shining *devas,* just like Nalakūvara and Maṇigrīva after their deliverance.

From all this we can safely repeat what Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura sings in his song *Gurudeva:* “When I examine myself, I find nothing of value. Your mercy is therefore essential to me.” Kṛṣṇa is in your hands, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Because your mercy is essential to us, all your instructions, *in toto,* are essential to us. Therefore, *vaiṣṇava ṭhākura tomāra kukkura*—our hope is to become your dog and try to carry out your will. Your will is sacred in all aspects, for by carrying it out Kṛṣṇa’s personal attention comes to us condemned beings who once made the original mistake of leaving Him.

Your servants at the Hare Krishna Center, Stockholm.  
(written by Tapas Dāsa)

## Sydney

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In two years your servants here at ISKCON Sydney will celebrate the 40th anniversary of your installing Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha on May 10th, 1971. You carried Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha to Australia on an airplane and installed them a few days later. Before leaving you told Them, “Now I am leaving you in the hands of the *mlecchas.* I cannot take the responsibility. You please guide these boys and girls and give them the intelligence to worship You nicely.” (*The Great Transcendental Adventure,* p. 84)

Since that day our temple has enjoyed immense successes, as well as series of tests. Devotees have matured and learned the science of Deity worship, thousands have been introduced to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, millions of your books have been distributed, and millions of hours of service have been rendered.

Your mission here in Sydney started, as usual, in a humble way. You inspired a few sincere pioneers to begin chanting and dancing, all the while tolerating prejudice. Your pure heart and love inspired them to do the impossible: protest against consumerism and give their lives to Kṛṣṇa.

Accounts of your first visit to Sydney show how dependent on Kṛṣṇa you were. There was no time for training your early followers or correcting their mistakes born of immaturity. It was simply your faith in the process and prayer to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha that saved those boys and girls, inspiring them to share your teachings with others and expand the movement.

Thanks to your blessing, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha continue to look after us here in Sydney. We understand that you did not travel so far to the shores of Australia so that we could simply live comfortably and enjoy your teachings for ourselves. We have inherited a great responsibility: to spread your teachings among new people while nurturing the *saṅga* of practicing devotees. The more we mature, the more we realize how important your mission is.

On this auspicious day we wish to express deep gratitude for your kindness in giving us the opportunity to serve your mission under Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha’s merciful shelter. We also wish to offer you a prayer to bestow on us a drop of your missionary spirit and compassion, which made your preaching so effective, so that we may share our good fortune with others.

Please keep us in your prayers and make us *mlecchas* worthy of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha’s love.

Your servants at ISKCON Sydney, Australia.  
(written by Vara-nāyaka Dāsa)

## Taipei

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your appearance! All glories to your transcendental teachings of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which perfectly follow the divine *paramparā* descending from Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu! All glories to your great personal example of unalloyed *bhakti* and *kṛṣṇa-prema*!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, although we never met you directly, we are attracted and enlightened by the beautiful teachings you introduced to the world outside India and to your gifts of the holy name and *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* which have the most wonderful tastes of anything in the world.

Hearing and following the teachings of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you have passed on to us not only clears up all our doubts but also cuts all the knots in our hearts, which have been accumulating for lifetimes.

Since time immemorial we have been wandering from planet to planet and species to species, but after hearing the message you have imparted we have dived into the amazing ocean of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This ocean is perfectly complete and extremely inconceivable. Only sincere, fortunate souls can receive and understand your mercy, which comes ultimately from Kṛṣṇa, and thus contact this most wonderful philosophy and most sublime way of life, a life in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

It is due to your compassion toward the fallen, conditioned souls and your love for Kṛṣṇa that you spread the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thus we are able to serve guru and Kṛṣṇa, turn our faces back toward Kṛṣṇa, break free from the clutches of illusion, and revive our dormant love of Kṛṣṇa.

Scientists and philosophers never succeed in their attempts to deny the transcendental existence and supremacy of God. Though they try by all means, they still break their heads by ignoring and rejecting the eternal, absolute nature of Kṛṣṇa. They are bewildered and frustrated. Their minds are turbulent. Their intelligence is covered. They just cannot admit that life comes from life, nor can they understand that we are God’s eternal parts and parcels.

Moreover, everyone has a unique eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa in the original spiritual abode. No one can negate the eternal absolute truth that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Person. The Supreme Personality of Godhead has all varieties of opulence in full, and we all have an eternal relationship with Him.

One who simply speculates with his limited mind and maintains an attitude of envy toward the very existence of God will always remain in the whirlpool of his own speculations and confusion, even after the annihilation of this phenomenal world.

But to those who want to face God, who want to be with Him and serve Him, He directly manifests Himself, and then we can comprehend and perceive Him in various ways.

As we progressively experience the chanting and hearing the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, we feel blissfully, transcendentally enlightened. When we give ourselves to the holy name, we cannot resist the joy and cheer coming from hearing and chanting. We just forget our external surroundings. This is just inconceivable.

Simple vegetarian food, after being cooked with devotion and offered through the *guru-paramparā* to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, becomes the most satisfying element in our existence. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not only spiritual but also so practical and powerful!

Your teachings and your personal example enlighten us in every aspect of life, both materially and spiritually. It is our great fortune to have received such transcendental knowledge from you, the pure, transcendental messenger of Kṛṣṇa.

To propagate Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Taiwan, the land of voidism and impersonalism, is very difficult because it requires a revolution in consciousness in a thoroughly mundane society. Without your mercy, we are helpless and hopeless.

Here we are in Taiwan, historically known as Formosa, meaning “the beautiful island.” Your representatives have been coming to Taiwan to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness for more than two decades, and we are slightly glad that we have a little result, though we are very slow. Though quite a number of local people have heard the name “Kṛṣṇa” and tasted *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and though millions of your books have been distributed, till now only a handful of devotees here have been initiated.

But where can there truly be a beautiful island in the material world, especially when Kali is speeding up his actions in the materialists’ minds? This island, Taiwan, is not spared from the invasions of the disease of materialism. The spirit souls here are suffering more and more, panicking from the relentless influence of Kali within and without. We need to work harder, cooperate better, and improve our personal and collective practices so we can transmit your mercy properly.

We sincerely pray for your mercy to protect us, guide us, and make us mature and strong in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We pray that we remain humble, faithful servants with grateful hearts, that we learn your teachings properly and following your instructions seriously. Then this small island of Taiwan, part of Kṛṣṇa’s creation, will really become a beautiful island of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this world.

Thank you again for your mercy. We pray to always remain humble servants of Your Divine Grace and Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya! Please accept our humble obeisances again!

Your servants at the Kṛṣṇa Foundation Society, ISKCON of Taipei, Taiwan, Republic of China.  
(written by Rāja-lakṣmī Devī Dāsī)

## Tucson

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I get the honor to write each year,  
To speak my heart, concise and clear.  
Much to do, but can’t delay—  
I wouldn’t have it any other way.  
It’s pushing 100, it’s starting to swelter,  
I think of your example as my shelter.  
Lost in thought of how to repay—  
I couldn’t learn austerity any other way.  
Dealing with problems, delusion, and debt;  
All part of my service, lest I forget.  
Your instructions give strength so I don’t sway—  
I couldn’t be dedicated any other way.  
Serving your mission and giving my all,  
Never giving up, though the results may be small,  
Following your program day after day—  
I wouldn’t know humility any other way.  
At times those you rely on for vision  
End up, instead, only causing division.  
Their association I keep at bay—  
I couldn’t be strong any other way.  
Hearing your lectures and urgency to preach,  
I’m also impelled to go out and teach  
Not my words but the words you say—  
I couldn’t imbibe knowledge any other way.  
How to attract more souls to this path,  
To rescue them from Māyās wrath,  
To show them love and how to pray—  
I couldn’t feel compassion any other way.  
Your mercy is soothing like sweet summer rain;  
Your pleasing glance is what I hope to attain;  
I bow at your feet; I’m determined to stay—  
I would be truly lost any other way.

Your servants in Tucson, Arizona, USA.  
(written by Sandāminī Devī Dāsī)

## Vancouver

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In Los Angeles you once said to Jayapatāka Mahārāja, “If you bring me a rose each day it will increase my longevity.”

ISKCON is yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so just as one offers Gaṅgā water to Mother Gaṅgā and *sūrya-praṇāmas* to the sun god, similarly, we would like to offer ISKCON to you in the form of a red rose.

**The Iskcon Rose**

O beautiful rose of velvet flesh,  
Whose veins like blood your petals test,  
By reaching to burst and spread and fall  
To perfume Earth with your fragrance.  
Long may your battle and cycle extend  
To erase the stench of the death of men,  
Who live in vain and die the same  
Without ever knowing that you exist.  
In vain they live and in vain they die.  
And those who love and mourn their loss  
Plant roses on their graves in grief,  
Only in death do they know of this vigil you keep.  
Do not despair, oh perfect bloom.  
Continue to fight with perfumed might,  
Matching velvet flesh against missile site.  
The Age of the Rose has thus begun.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, on behalf of your Vancouver disciples, granddisciples, and great-granddisciples, we thank you for all the mercy bestowed by your ISKCON upon each one of us, and we offer you roses of gratitude and love on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

Your servants in Vancouver, Canada.  
(written by Padyāvalī Devī Dāsī)

## Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your merciful lotus feet!

This year, along with my own deepest appreciation and gratitude, I would like to offer Your Divine Grace my son’s appreciation and gratitude. Sometimes, while reading some of the letters you wrote to your disciples, I notice how much affection and care you had for our children and how happy you were to know the progress they were making in their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You wanted us *gṛhasthas* to provide all facilities and good examples for our sons and daughters, so that they could become Kṛṣṇa conscious under their parents’ protection, be liberated in this very lifetime, and also set an example for others in the future. Therefore, remembering how you encouraged us and how much you appreciated those of us who tried our best to raise Kṛṣṇa conscious children, I am sure you will be very pleased to hear the offering that one representative of our new generation, who has just turned nineteen, has written in honor of this most auspicious occasion of your appearance day.

Glancing at the world outside of me, I feel more and more grateful for the immense mercy carried by Your Divine Grace. Just like the sun that rises every day to shine upon mankind, you shine in the heart of every devotee, giving us the strength to progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If I stop for a moment and just think about what my life would be like without Kṛṣṇa and Your Divine Grace, I immediately feel disgusted and very anguished and entrapped, as if I were in a spire of hopeless illusion. Therefore I don’t think I will ever be able to thank you enough for the tireless work and heavy sacrifices you underwent to help poor souls like me, lost in this material world. You are the real democratic personality because your message is universal and given without any discrimination, touching the heart of every living being and making him feel genuine spiritual joy.

In this world, where abuse has taken the place of justice, where irreligion is spreading like an epidemic, where the decline in morals is disguised as liberalism, and man has come to the point of believing himself to be God, your message is the only shelter, the only anchor for mankind adrift. As Kali-yuga progresses, we see the gradual destruction of all spiritual values and the increase of all kinds of violence; therefore how could we possibly hope to conquer this tragedy without the help your transcendental support, be it in the form of your books, your personal instructions, or the example of your pastimes in this world, as told to us by your dedicated disciples?

As far as I am concerned, I will be eternally grateful to Your Divine Grace for rescuing me from a miserable and ineffectual life. Recently, after some ups and downs and some health problems, I had the good fortune to realize what a great treasure you have given all of us, and by your mercy I am learning not to throw away the time that is allotted to us in this human form of life. Often you used to warned us, “Don’t waste time, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa!” It would be foolish on my part to not admit that it is difficult to keep the mind fixed on the beautiful form of Śyāmasundara during the whole day, because I am often busy with some sort of material activity or am still looking for some sort of sense gratification. But more and more I am remembering your teachings, and then I can withdraw into myself, at least for some time, and feel overwhelmed by an ocean of transcendental nectar. In a very sweet and incontrovertible way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have changed my life.

Even though I was born in a devotee family, for quite a long time I was dazzled and led astray by material illusion. Days and days passed by without meaning. Years went by, and as I studied my schoolbooks I became proud of my material knowledge and arrogant. My mind was disturbed and frustrated by the inebriating material pleasures, and I was convinced that the speculative philosophies I was learning in school were the real truth. In this way I neglected Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy and all the activities that gravitate around the temple. From my mouth only expressions of futility would come out, and quickly I found myself caught in a storm of ignorance, lust, frustration, and indolence.

Then one day, by the causeless mercy of the Lord, I received a good deal of physical suffering, and it was that very suffering that made me come near to your divine teachings and experience their sweetness! As soon as I began reading your *Bhagavad-gītā* and chanting the *mahā-mantra,* I felt happier than I had ever felt before. That joy was of a different nature—deeper, real, complete. Now I understand that life has a fundamental goal: devotional service to guru and Kṛṣṇa.

Since then I am trying to dedicate all my free time to chanting the holy name and reading about the glories of the Lord, which you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have made it easy for us to understand. In this way everything I do makes sense, because I do it in the context of serving the Lord.

Even though I am young and unsteady, I pray from the core of my heart that Your Divine Grace may help me walk in a steadfast way and overcome the numerous obstacles on my path. But above all I pray that you give me the strength to hold on to your lotus feet under any circumstances. I also pray for all the young people who today feel very much disturbed by the innumerable dangers of this world; I pray that one day—soon, I hope—they can also taste a drop of the nectar of devotional service taught us by you, our great founder-*ācārya,* and by doing so they will stop, once and for all, the frightening repetition of birth and death.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Jaya* Jagat-guru!

*Jaya* Patita-pāvana!

The devotees at Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa, near Milan, Italy.  
(written by Ālī-Kṛṣṇa Devī Dāsī and Nitāi-prasāda Dāsa)

## Vṛndāvana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our grateful and humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The world is most fortunate to have had the touch of your lotus feet. You moved through this world at such a pace, with your smiling lotus face . . . sprinkling the gift of *kṛṣṇa-prema* on the hearts of the entire human race . . . We thank you for your sacrifice.

To understand you, we must pray to understand the depth of your compassion, which is a bottomless, ever-expanding ocean. You sacrificed your life to give the suffering *jīvas* of this world the elixir of immortality. Not just any elixir, but the sweetest elixir of immortality, our eternal relationship with Śrī Kṛṣṇa in Śrī Vṛndāvana.

You left us an invaluable inheritance. This inheritance is for all generations, for all devotees, for all time. To qualify for it one simply has to accept you as our real father or grandfather, as the case may be. This inheritance of your estate takes the form of ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa. When this estate is properly claimed, we can then effectively give this precious gift to others.

We must develop greed for this inheritance. This greed is not our enemy but the eagerness to carry out the simple order of Guru and Gaurāṅga, to personally pursue Kṛṣṇa with great enthusiasm, and then to share that Kṛṣṇa consciousness with others. This is the best method of claiming the inheritance that you have given. We can then receive the blessings of the father in full.In the same way, Lord Caitanya freely distributed immortal garlands of *hari-nāma-prema.*

*sei dvāre ācaṇḍāle kīrtana sañcāre*  
*nāma-prema-mālā gāṅthi’ parāila saṁsāre*

*ei-mata bhakta-bhāva kari’ aṅgīkāra*  
*āpani ācari’ bhakti karila pracāra*

**Thus He spread** ***kīrtana*** **even among the untouchables. He wove a wreath of the holy name and** ***prema,*** **with which He garlanded the entire material world. In this way, assuming the sentiment of a devotee, He preached devotional service while practicing it Himself. \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā*** **4.40–41\]**

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, procured an unlimited number of these garlands from Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, enough to distribute for the next ten thousand years, and gave them out to anyone who would simply accept them. You began distributing these garlands on the frigid streets of New York, a city of hearts made cold by many winters of disappointment. You gave them out with great care and attention. One elderly Bengali gentleman with garlands . . . singing in the Bowery . . . changing the world forever. What are the odds?

Some ruffians accepted, motley crew,  
Just enough to see it through . . .  
Stealing their hearts with *kṛṣṇa-prem*,  
Conquering the world with the holy name.  
The world you encircled a dozen times,  
Garlands unlimited, you were that kind.  
No one was qualified, yet none were denied  
Access to Vṛndāvana—“Please come inside.”  
Your kindness and love are a shoreless ocean;  
You loved us and calmed our hearts’ commotion.  
Now we have inherited these garlands . . . what to do?  
Yes, to one degree or another we owe that motley crew.  
We owe it to them and especially to you. Shouldn’t we see what we can do?  
The *jīvas* of this world are waiting for their garlands, too.  
We can’t be as pure as you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, yet we can strive  
To distribute those garlands and see your mission thrive.  
It’s our duty, our love’s expression,  
To make a pure devotional impression.  
Prabhupāda, you have given us all from within,  
The chance to cleanse these hearts from sin.  
There is no better way to please you than to expand your mission,  
To help you distribute *kṛṣṇa-prema* with the same selfless passion.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for Śrī Vṛndāvana.

The devotees trying to serve you at ISKCON Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Gadī Dāsa)

## Washington, D. C.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you! All glories to this wonderful association of Vaiṣṇavas that you have created.

Last year in November, a friend had a dream about you. You sat in a transcendental shop selling everything one needed to live in Vṛndāvana. This devotee bought many items and asked how much to pay. You responded that there was no need to. She protested that she could not take so much for free. “Pay what you can,” you said in a compassionate voice. Hesitatingly, she dug out a crumpled fifty-rupee note from her pocket and, embarrassed, offered it. Then you sent her away, saying “Just take the items and go.”

This is our situation, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You’ve given us all that we need to live in Vṛndāvana, and just as in the dream, what can we ever hope to pay for it? We have indeed received unlimited gifts from you. The gift of your transcendental books, which can dispel the darkness in the darkest of hearts; *kīrtana;* festivals; a worldwide family of devotees; and the many communities you started that bring all of this together. Underlying all these gifts is a deep-rooted desire and broad vision to connect with everyone in this world and share these gifts with them.

New Hastināpura Dhāma, located in Potomac, a suburb of Washington, D.C., the capital of America, one of the most powerful cities in the world, is a place brimming with great potential. It is a place from which we can share Kṛṣṇa and your wonderful gifts with the world’s elite and powerful. Interestingly enough, in the past Potomac was also spelled Patomack and Petomeack, an Algonquian Indian word meaning “a meeting place” or “a trading place.” It is no coincidence that the first Deity in ISKCON, Śrī Madana-mohana, desired to reside here in Potomac, the meeting place. Madana-mohana is the Deity for *sambandha,* or reviving our original relationship with Kṛṣṇa. His presence therefore places a great responsibility on us not only to help others rekindle their relationship with Him but also to deepen our connection with each other. You desired that we create such beautiful gardens that the senators’ wives would ask their husbands to take them there for a stroll. If you have built a house where the whole world can live, Potomac certainly has the potential to be an important reception area.

As we approach this beautiful gift, however, we are awed and sometimes challenged by the multifaceted nature of our community: the young, the old, the white, brown, and black, men, women, first generation, second generation, the conservatives and the liberals. Sometimes, this diversity seems daunting and an obstacle in moving forward. However, this diversity, a unique contribution of yours, is an essential aspect of the personal feature of Kṛṣṇa. It is an expression of His beautiful and variegated nature. But impersonalists that we are, we reject these differences and instead desire homogeneity and ultimately oneness. “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate” was one of your final instructions. While trying to cooperate, we often stumble on our differences and get distracted. Valuable time and energy are lost as a result. How do we turn this perceived obstacle into our strength?

You taught us that Kṛṣṇa is a person in whom all contradictions are resolved. He is heavier than the heaviest, lighter than the lightest, the biggest and the smallest, all at the same time. This is the *acintya-śakti* of the absolute Divine.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a perfect representative of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, you reconcile all these diversities perfectly. You were in your late sixties when you started the movement yet had the vitality and youthful energy of a twenty-year-old. You were strict in preaching the *siddhānta* yet very tolerant of the shortcomings of others. You were conservative in preserving and presenting the message of the previous *ācāryas* yet liberal in its application. Even though a traditional Bengali from Calcutta, you were comfortable mixing and interacting with the Western youth. Understanding the broad nature of your divine personality is one of the keys to understanding and appreciating differences that we see in our community members. Just as white light in contact with a prism breaks into many different colors, similarly your personality is refracted in many colors in your different followers, each color equally beautiful and pleasing to Kṛṣṇa. To love you completely means to love all these attributes of yours refracted in your dedicated followers. True unity thus means to accept every unique flower growing in your transcendental garden, string a beautiful multicolored garland, and offer it at your lotus feet.

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON of Washington, D.C., Potomac, Maryland, USA.  
(written by Vidarbha-suta Devī Dāsī)

## Yogyakarta

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Guru and ŚrīGaurāṅga!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would like to thank you again and again for your causeless mercy upon us. You provided us with such a vast ocean of transcendental knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in your books. Your books are a matchless gift, especially for us here in Yogyakarta, a city well known all over Indonesia for its religious tolerance. More and more people from various religious backgrounds come to our *āśrama* to learn of the philosophy and practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Again, your books are a priceless source of knowledge that we can share with those who are interested in the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the Vedic philosophy in general.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our humble request is that you please kindly be more merciful to us so that we can always get the chance to spread the holy name of Kṛṣṇa among the educated people here in Yogyakarta. Please give us strength so that we may be able to maintain our devotional service to our beloved Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara despite our limitations in many ways. Being fulltime students and following a good daily *sādhana* at the same time is a challenge that is not always easy for us to meet. Your lotus feet are the only shelter for us. Śrīla Prabhupāda, may your glories spread all over the three worlds.

Your servants at Nārāyaṇa Smṛti Āśrama, ISKCON Yogyakarta, Indonesia.  
(written by Sūrya Locana Dāsa)

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homage from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you, our guiding light in life’s journey across the vast ocean of nescience!

I beg Your Divine Grace for inspiration as I make this attempt to glorify you, a great Vaiṣṇava representative of Vyāsadeva. Although I have no qualification, I will offer something from my heart and pray you accept it.

In my attempt to glorify you, I would be remiss in my duty if I did not make the effort to rightly glorify your disciples here at the BBT who have taken the *saṅkīrtana* mission as their life and soul. Your potency, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is most evident as I watch with amazement how these devotees, for decades, have not given up the fight and gone away. They are so devoted to your mission that they endure many hardships to help dispel the dense darkness of irreligion by printing and distributing your transcendental literature. Their service is unmotivated and uninterrupted, their only desire being to please you and, in so doing, to please the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

In a letter written in 1839, the American philosopher Ralph Waldo Emerson wrote, “The apple never falls far from the stem.” Here Emerson is describing the tug that often brings us back to our childhood home. As Vaiṣṇavas, we can understand this saying to mean that no one should remain in this temporary world, full of miseries, but should go back home, back to Godhead. You came to this Western world to renew our eternal relationship with the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa and take us home. Your books are the branches of the tree of Vedic knowledge, and by attaching ourselves to you through your words we feel the tug that will one day help us transcend this material existence and go back home, back to Godhead.

Who in this material world is not suffering? Every day we receive calls and letters from people in mental institutions, prisons, and hospitals, from the elderly and sick. And youth is no bar against suffering: many young or relatively young people have had their hearts broken so that formerly sweet relationships are dead, or their homes are in turmoil, or they’re strung out on drugs or alcohol. And everywhere there is the thick malaise brought on by the worldwide economic crisis. The fire of material life is burning everyone who comes to this material world. If we think that just a little enjoyment apart from Kṛṣṇa will not burn us, we’re like the fool who lights just the corner of a pan of gasoline, hoping not get scorched. Everyone here is suffering, and only those who are fortunate enough to come in contact with a devotee who has taken to the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness will finally have their questions answered and feel some cooling relief. As you wrote, “The Vedic instructions are like nectar and cool water for one suffering from scorching heat.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.12.3)

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bringing us devotional service and giving us the answers to the questions that have plagued mankind for centuries. We hear these questions all the time. Why does a loving God permit war? Why is there disease? Why do babies die? Why do bad things happen to good people, and, even more interesting to some, why do good things happen to bad people? Thank you for giving us not only a vast storehouse of knowledge through your books but a way to disseminate this knowledge to all the conditioned souls.

You are *patita-pāvana,* the most compassionate servant of the Lord and the greatest benefactor of human society. Because of you, the entire society has been given a way to progress toward the highest perfection of life. We must take advantage of this great benediction.

Though my role here at the BBT is insignificant, I feel intense gratitude for the mercy you rained down on my head to wake me up to a greater purpose and plan for a life that would have otherwise ended in despair. I thank Kṛṣṇa for bringing me to the devotees who have followed in your footsteps, and I thank you for bringing us Kṛṣṇa.

Knowing that your success came from your unmotivated and uninterrupted service to your Guru Mahārāja, we pray that we may continuously serve your mission so that it will go on and on and one day be handed over to other souls who have also, somehow or other, received your mercy.

Your words are clear as crystal: “There is nothing new to be said. Whatever I had to say I have already said in my books. Now you must all try to understand it and continue with your endeavors.” (*Śrī Nāmāmṛta,* Preface)

All glories to the *saṅkīrtana* movement! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day!

Your servants at the North American offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, Los Angeles, California, USA.  
(written by Dvārakārāṇī Devī Dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In a remote and cold corner of the world, in the Northern Hemisphere, a small group of devotees feel truly blessed. We feel like every day is an offering at your feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Surely all devotees feel the same, but we feel especially lucky because we get to work directly with and for your books. Every day.

Luckily there are so many of your divine books and so many languages into which they need to be translated that we will be busy for the rest of our lives. At times the task seems overwhelming, but that feeling is soon overridden by another feeling, the feeling of gratitude for the opportunity to serve you in this way. For this we thank you.

To work on your books feels like getting your very close association. Most of us were not around during your glorious presence, but now we get to make up for that by helping you spread your books all over the globe. At the end of each day we know that our efforts are worth something and that we are part of something revolutionary and important. As we sit in our small offices and work on translations, graphic design, layout, etc., we are always aware of our transcendental boss—you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are always present in everything we do.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

With great gratitude, humility, and eagerness to serve you, we remain  
Your devotees at the northern European offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, Korsnās Gṛrd, Sweden.  
(written by Puṣkariṇī Devī Dāsī)

## Congregational Development Ministry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We, your servants at the ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry, are honored to humbly assist your mission, which includes spiritualizing the homes of whoever connects with ISKCON. You wrote:

Members of the household should learn to worship the deity as we are doing, following the regulative principles, then it will be successful. We want every house to be a temple, not that our place should be the only place for the people to come. \[Letter, 14 April 1972\]

You envisioned that such homes would develop as reference points for seekers of spiritual knowledge and would function as representatives of ISKCON. You wrote:

It is not that we have to open different centers all over the world. Whoever cares for the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement can install Deities at home and, under superior guidance, worship the Deity regularly, chanting the *mahā-mantra* and discussing the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* We are actually teaching in our classes how to go about this. \[*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya-līlā* 3.190, purport\]

In fact, your own example in your *gṛhastha* years remains a bright beacon of inspiration and reference.

From a recent survey of twenty-three ISKCON initiating spiritual masters, we learned that ninety-six percent of their disciples are now home-based. Over the past forty years, ISKCON has undergone a wide social transformation. So if they want to remain relevant to the changing circumstances within ISKCON’s devotional communities, your servants—especially those who provide leadership within ISKCON—need to carefully study the new situation and identify the enormous potential within ISKCON’s congregations.

You wanted a movement of gurus, and home-based devotees are certainly included in your plans. You said:

That is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction. . . . You deliver your village people and become a guru. Everyone is not going to be so big that he can go all over the world. But everyone can teach within his limit—within his family, within his community, within his village, within his town, within his district. As he is capable, he can increase. But everyone can become a guru and deliver the local people. \[Room conversation, Bombay, 21 August 1975\]

One cultural obstruction to seeing your vision fulfilled is the notion that home-based devotees are a kind of “second-class citizens” of your Society. Although you never introduced terms such as “part-time devotee” (as if adherence to *bhakti-yoga* were a job) or “external devotee” (as if one’s place of residence determines one’s devotional status), this language still plagues your Society. Such demeaning expressions, residues of the mentality that only temple devotees are true devotees, should disappear from the lexicon of your followers. In fact, even in the days when almost all devotees of ISKCON were temple residents, you wrote:

Actually there is no difference between devotees living inside the temple and devotees living outside the temple. \[Letter, 29 September 1972\]

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! May your inclusive vision for elevating everyone to the stage of being a spiritual shelter for others be established all over the world.

Your servants at the ISKCON Congregational Development Ministry.

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, our most beloved spiritual master and our most trusted well-wisher and friend.

Recently we have been having some difficult times in this material world. For so many years we have been able to push on, to the best of our ability, your message of cow protection. As we awoke each day we thought, “We are being allowed to serve the cows again for another day. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

Although our service seems to us to be less than perfect, and we always seem to be wishing we could be more potent, Lord Kṛṣṇa still allows us to serve the cows. This past year has been a reminder that it is only through Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy that we can continue this service. Due to the increased severity of Balabhadra Prabhu’s back problems, to the point of imprisonment in bed for two months and subsequent heart attack, we have been seriously reminded of how easily we can be separated from serving Lord Kṛṣṇa’s beloved cows.

Balabhadra was incapacitated, and our hands-on backup and office help, our daughter Lakshmi, broke her wrist falling on the ice on the way to the barn to serve the cows in Balabhadra’s absence. She had surgery, and for three months afterward she could not use her right hand and arm. Not only could she not do anything for herself, but she could not serve the cows in the capacity of either cowherd or office help. During most of the severe winter, the two cowherds could not serve the cows. This left Chāya Devī, who usually does only office work, as the main cowherd. We prayed to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for strength. You heard our prayers, and the cows received good care in the absence of their favorite cowherds.

Help came not only in the form of hands-on physical assistance, but also in the form of the heartfelt letters, phone calls, and visits from your devotees from all over the world. How little did we realize how immense a community of followers you have created with your loving example of pure love for Lord Kṛṣṇa! We have only received such attention and encouragement because we are an active part of this community that is dedicated to you. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the inspiration encouraging these loving exchanges. Your exchange with us in the role of ever well-wisher and trusted friend is also the basis for our loving exchanges with the cows.

In our last report to you, we told you of Nanda the trained ox and how he had fallen due to long-term chronic nerve problems in his back legs.
